### ADM-09 · MEDIUM · Admin builds always target production, so every branch preview runs unreviewed code on prod

- **Slice:** Admin application (admin.vyact.app) · **Category:** ops-release · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `admin/.env.production:5`, `admin/.env.production:6`, `admin/src/lib/supabase.ts:12`, `admin/src/lib/supabase.ts:14`, `admin/src/lib/supabase.ts:15`, `admin/vercel.json:3`, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:32`
- **Evidence:** Vite loads the committed admin/.env.production for every `vite build`, preview builds included. On top of that, supabase.ts falls back to the production URL and key in any PROD build: `(import.meta.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL ...) || (import.meta.env.PROD ? FALLBACK_URL : undefined)` (supabase.ts:14-15). Unlike react/vite.config.ts there is no preview or VITE_LOCAL_ONLY guard. docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:32 states "Previews of any other branch never touch production data", but that guard exists only for the consumer. CLAUDE.md records the same class of bug for the consumer (PUB-026: "until v10.57.0 every preview silently ran on production").
- **Failure scenario:** A PR that changes admin/src/lib/contentApi.ts (for example a buggy bulk-update, or a malicious dependency bump) gets a Vercel preview build. An admin opens the preview link to review it and signs in. The unreviewed code now runs with that admin's production god-mode JWT and can write content_items, or read every ledger, before the PR is merged.
- **Recommendation:** Mirror the consumer's preview guard: in admin/vite.config.ts, fail the build (or blank the Supabase config) when VERCEL_ENV === 'preview' and the branch is not staging/develop. Remove the PROD fallback constants from supabase.ts. Give previews the vyact-test env. Add a PUB-026-style test that parses admin/src/lib/supabase.ts and vite.config.ts for the guard.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The committed admin/.env.production:5-6 points every vite build at the prod project, and supabase.ts:14-15 adds a PROD fallback. admin/vite.config.ts has no VERCEL_ENV preview guard, unlike react/vite.config.ts:17-29. Only staging/develop get branch-scoped overrides (ENVIRONMENTS.md:28-30). Password sign-in (admin auth.ts:10) works on any preview origin, so unmerged admin code runs with a prod admin JWT.

## Backend

### BE-FN-02 · HIGH · Scheduled 30-day deletion never executes, and signing in after the deadline silently cancels it

- **Slice:** Other Edge Functions (delete-account, email, web-push) · **Category:** compliance · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S6 ('Scheduled deletion is not demonstrably automated'). Confirmed still open. The post-deadline cancellation is new.
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:3-6`, `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:57-64`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:181-196`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:242-251`, `react/src/components/auth/AuthGate.tsx:74`, `supabase/migrations/20260701120000_v98_privacy_deletion_controls.sql:37-38`, `supabase/migrations/20260701120000_v98_privacy_deletion_controls.sql:116-132`
- **Evidence:** request_account_deletion only stamps `deletion_scheduled_for = now() + 30 days` (v98:116-132). The only thing that purges is the delete-account function, which needs the user's own JWT (index.ts:44-46). Its only caller is Settings.onDeleteImmediately (Settings.tsx:206-207). No pg_cron job, worker or service-role path exists: the cron jobs in migrations are whatsapp-dispatch-* and web-push-dispatch only. Meanwhile AuthGate calls `reactivateIfNeeded()` on every sign-in (AuthGate.tsx:74). That function clears deletion_requested_at whenever it is set (auth.ts:247-249), with no check that the deadline has passed. The UI tells the user 'After 30 days this cannot be undone' (Settings.tsx:183).
- **Failure scenario:** A user schedules deletion on 1 Oct and is signed out. On 31 Oct nothing runs, and the account and all household data stay in production indefinitely. If the user signs in on 15 Dec to check, the request is erased with a 'Welcome back' toast. A request under privacy law (DPDP/GDPR) is never fulfilled, while the product says it will be.
- **Recommendation:** Add a daily pg_cron job that calls a service-role purge of every profile with `deletion_scheduled_for <= now()`, sharing the purge RPC proposed in the FK finding. Change reactivate_my_account to clear the request only while `deletion_scheduled_for > now()`. Record completion evidence, for example a minimal deletion_log row with a user-id hash and timestamp. Tests: a SQL test that sets deletion_scheduled_for in the past, runs the purge and asserts the rows are gone, and a unit test that reactivateIfNeeded does not clear an expired request.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The only cron.schedule entries are whatsapp-dispatch-* and web-push-dispatch; no workflow schedules a purge. delete-account is called only from Settings.tsx:206-207 (immediate). reactivate_my_account (v98:99-110) clears the request with no deadline check, and AuthGate.tsx:74 runs it on every session. A scheduled deletion never executes, and a later sign-in cancels it, despite the 'after 30 days' copy (Settings.tsx:1…
- **Also reported as:** INT-HH-04 (Scheduled deletion never runs; deleting an account destroys co-members' shared households); XC-PRV-05 (Scheduled 30-day deletion never executes, and the hold is not enforced server-side)

### BE-FN-07 · HIGH · One failing push endpoint aborts the whole web-push dispatch, which also does not scale

- **Slice:** Other Edge Functions (delete-account, email, web-push) · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/_shared/web-push.ts:304-309`, `supabase/functions/_shared/web-push.ts:320-342`, `supabase/functions/web-push/index.ts:46-55`, `supabase/functions/web-push/index.ts:120-176`, `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:63-96`, `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:77`, `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:184`
- **Evidence:** `sendWebPush` awaits `fetchImpl(target.endpoint, ...)` outside any try (web-push.ts:332), with no timeout or AbortSignal. In `dispatch`, the per-device loop (index.ts:160-172) has no try/catch either, so a DNS, TLS or reset error propagates to the handler's catch and returns 500 `dispatch_failed` (50-54). The `pushed_at` stamp at line 175 is then never reached. The allowlist accepts wildcard subdomains (`[a-z0-9-]+\.notify\.windows\.com`, `[a-z0-9-]+\.push\.apple\.com`, both in web-push.ts:307-308 and the RPC regex at migration:77), so any user can register an endpoint on a host that does not resolve, with valid-looking P-256 keys. register_push_subscription has no per-user or per-household cap (63-96). Everything runs serially in one invocation: every household's ledger load, every send, plus `.in('household_id', households)` lists that grow with the user base (128, 155). A payload over 3,000 bytes (`payload_too_large`, web-push.ts:260) counts as a device failure, and 5 of those disable a healthy device (migration:184).
- **Failure scenario:** A user registers `https://nx-0000.notify.windows.com/w/x` and pushes their own budget to 80%, which raises a P1. From then on, every 15-minute run throws on that fetch. Devices processed earlier are re-pushed the same notifications every run for 24 h, and no household processed later ever gets a lock-screen push. The bad subscription is never retired, because record() and the 5-failure rule are skipped. A slow push service alone has the same effect.
- **Recommendation:** Wrap each send in try/catch with `AbortSignal.timeout(10_000)` and treat a network error as a failed delivery (recorded and counted). Record delivery per notification and subscription instead of a global stamp at the end. Restrict wildcard hosts to known WNS/APNs patterns or resolve them before registering. Cap subscriptions at about 10 per user. Batch households (pagination, or a queue with one invocation per N households). Do not count payload_too_large against the device; truncate the title and body. Test: a vitest test of dispatch with a fetchImpl that throws for one sub, asserting the others are pushed and pushed_at is stamped.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): sendWebPush calls fetchImpl outside any try and with no timeout (_shared/web-push.ts:155). The dispatch push loop has no try/catch (web-push/index.ts:160-172), so one throw returns 500 and the pushed_at stamp at :175 is skipped. Earlier devices are then re-pushed every 15 min, and a bad subscription is never retired. Any user can register a wildcard *.notify.windows.com host (migration:77). Cited line numbers are wr…

### BE-WA-01 · HIGH · Removed members keep WhatsApp read access: Pip answers and scheduled pushes skip membership checks

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S1 (partially resolved: write RPCs revalidate membership; WhatsApp reads and proactive sends still do not)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:62-72`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:448-452`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:1076-1090`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/householdLoader.ts:30-55`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-dispatch/index.ts:64-86`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-send.ts:81-86`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:461-470`, `supabase/migrations/20260908120000_audit_s1_whatsapp_identities.sql:34-49`
- **Evidence:** `whatsapp_identities` (profile_id, phone_number, household_id) is written only by verify-otp and deleted only by the explicit `unlink` action. `leave_household` (baseline:470) only runs `delete from memberships ...`, the owner-removes-member RLS delete does the same, and no trigger cleans up the identity. Writes are safe: every write RPC re-checks `memberships`. Reads and sends do not. (1) `processInbound` only checks `profile.whatsapp_household_id` (webhook:448). `answerQuestion` then calls `loadHouseholdRows(supabase /*service role*/, profile.id, householdId)`, which drains every transaction, budget, debt, asset, account and schedule `.eq('household_id', householdId)` with no membership check (householdLoader:16-27). (2) whatsapp-dispatch builds its audience from `whatsapp_identities.select('profile_id, household_id')` (dispatch:65), and alerts, digest, weekly, month close, runway and bills-tomorrow all go to those members. `guardedSend` only checks `recipient.household_id !== householdId` (send:84), never `memberships`. Only `billsFor` filters approvers through `memberships`.
- **Failure scenario:** A couple shares a Family household. A has WhatsApp linked with ANSWERS ON. B removes A from the household after a separation. A sends "how much have we spent this month?" or "what's our net worth?" and Pip answers from B's full ledger, including account names and balances. Every evening A also gets `household_daily_digest` ("Priya ₹3,200 · you ₹0"), large-spend alerts naming B's accounts, the month-close summary and runway notes, indefinitely. The 2026-09 audit S1 noted that revoked members were affected, but only the write side was fixed.
- **Recommendation:** Make membership part of the identity's validity. (a) Add an AFTER DELETE trigger on `memberships` (and a role-change check) that deletes or nulls the matching `whatsapp_identities` row. (b) Defence in depth: in `processInbound`, and in `guardedSend` before any send, require a live `memberships` row for (profile_id, household_id); skip with `not_a_member` if there is none. (c) In whatsapp-dispatch, join identities to memberships when building `byHousehold`. Add DB tests (leave_household, then expect identity gone) and an Edge test: a removed member asks a question and gets the blocked reply; dispatch with a removed member plans 0 sends.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): No migration deletes whatsapp_identities on membership removal; its FKs only cascade on profile or household delete (audit_s1:34-40). processInbound gates on identity.household_id alone (webhook:448). answerQuestion → loadHouseholdRows drains the household with the service role and no membership check (householdLoader:30-39). guardedSend checks only recipient.household_id (whatsapp-send:84). Dispatch audience comes …
- **Also reported as:** BE-PIP-01 (WhatsApp link outlives household membership: removed members keep getting Pip answers and…); INT-HH-02 (Removed or departed members keep WhatsApp read access and household alerts)

### BE-FN-04 · MEDIUM · Owner deletion destroys co-members' households through a non-atomic sequence of deletes

- **Slice:** Other Edge Functions (delete-account, email, web-push) · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S6 (items 3 'shared-household rules' and 4 'transactional purge'). Still open.
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:13-16`, `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:33-36`, `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:74-86`
- **Evidence:** For every household where the caller's role is owner, the function deletes all 11 household tables, then `memberships`, then `households` (74-86). It does this as separate PostgREST calls with no transaction and no check for other members. A transfer_ownership RPC exists (baseline migration) but is never offered or used. A failure part-way (for example `households` failing after transactions are already deleted) returns 500 with data already gone, and the retry starts from a household with no transactions.
- **Failure scenario:** A Family household has the owner and 3 members, 2 years of history and about 5,000 transactions. When the owner deletes their account, all 3 members lose the shared ledger, budgets, accounts and debts without notice and with no chance to take ownership. If the households delete fails transiently after line 79 has run for transactions, the ledger is gone, the account still exists, and Net Worth reads 0 for every member.
- **Recommendation:** When other members exist, refuse with `transfer_required` (or transfer automatically to the longest-standing admin through transfer_ownership) and delete only the user's own membership. Do the erase in one SECURITY DEFINER RPC, `delete_owned_household(h)`, so it is all-or-nothing (deleting the households row cascades every child table). Test: a SQL test that runs the purge on a 2-member household and asserts it raises transfer_required and that no rows were deleted.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): index.ts:71-86 deletes every owned household with no member check and no transaction, so co-members lose the ledger. This is documented intent, though ('every household you own', Settings.tsx:183, index.ts:13-14), and an owner can already DELETE the household through RLS (baseline:762). The non-atomic risk is transient-only: child FKs are SET NULL or CASCADE (money_map_phase1:58-59, v91:92-93) and no table blocks th…

### BE-FN-06 · MEDIUM · send-split-email is an open spam/phishing relay from hello@vyact.app, with HTML injection via currency

- **Slice:** Other Edge Functions (delete-account, email, web-push) · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit §8.5 (spam and rate limits). Still open. The HTML injection and the brand-domain sender are new.
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/send-split-email/index.ts:142-187`, `supabase/functions/send-split-email/index.ts:50`, `supabase/functions/_shared/emailTemplates.ts:13-14`, `supabase/functions/_shared/emailTemplates.ts:29`, `supabase/functions/_shared/emailTemplates.ts:64-70`, `supabase/migrations/20260724130000_shared_splits.sql:57-67`, `supabase/migrations/20260724130000_shared_splits.sql:147-149`
- **Evidence:** Any signed-up user can insert a shared_splits row with free-text `currency text not null` (no CHECK, :63), any description, and any number of shares with any `email` (the insert policy only checks `owns_shared_split`, :147-149). The 'shared' event emails every share on every call (index.ts:165-187). Nothing records that a share was already emailed and there is no per-user or per-day cap. `money()` writes an unknown currency code raw (`${amt} ${cur}`, emailTemplates:14) into HTML lines that `renderEmail` does not escape (:29), while description and names are escaped. The sender name comes from the attacker-settable `user_metadata.full_name` (index.ts:143) and appears in the subject and heading. v10.60 makes the sender `Vyact <hello@vyact.app>` (index.ts:50).
- **Failure scenario:** An attacker sets full_name 'Vyact Security'. They create a split with currency `<a href="https://evil.example">Verify your bank account</a>` and 500 victim addresses, then call the function 100 times. That sends 50,000 brand-signed (DKIM vyact.app) emails with a live phishing link. vyact.app's sender reputation and the free Resend quota (3,000 a month) are burnt, and legitimate split and auth email stops arriving. The function is inert today because no provider is configured, but it becomes exploitable the moment the owner completes docs/EMAIL_TEMPLATES.md §0.
- **Recommendation:** Escape `cur` in money(), or better, whitelist ISO-4217 with a CHECK `currency ~ '^[A-Z]{3}$'` on shared_splits. Add `emailed_at` to shared_split_shares and send 'shared' only to shares where it is null. Cap shares per split (for example 20) and emails per user per day (count in a send log). For 'settled', require `paid = true`; for 'closed', require `closed_at` to be set. Test: a template unit test that currency `<b>x</b>` renders `&lt;b&gt;` and a handler test that a second 'shared' call sends 0.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): The code claims hold. The caller controls the share emails (shared_splits.sql:148), and currency is free text (:64) with no later CHECK. money() writes an unknown code raw (emailTemplates.ts:13-14) into unescaped lines (:29). 'shared' re-sends to everyone on every call, with no cap (index.ts:165-187). It is not exploitable today, because no provider is configured: emailConfigured() is false (index.ts:41,146) and doc…
- **Also reported as:** INT-HH-11 (send-split-email lets any user send unlimited email to arbitrary addresses); XC-PRV-13 (send-split-email lets any user mail arbitrary addresses repeatedly from vyact.app)

### BE-FN-09 · MEDIUM · People added to a split while editing are promised an invite that is never sent

- **Slice:** Other Edge Functions (delete-account, email, web-push) · **Category:** ux · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/components/splits/SplitFormModal.tsx:268-276`, `react/src/components/splits/SplitFormModal.tsx:329-331`, `react/src/lib/splitParticipants.ts:40`, `react/src/store/slices/sharedSplitsSlice.ts:78-103`, `react/src/store/slices/sharedSplitsSlice.ts:119`, `react/src/store/slices/sharedSplitsSlice.ts:133`
- **Evidence:** On edit, new emails go through the v10.59 confirmation ('… isn't on Vyact yet. Send them an invite to join and settle this split?', splitParticipants.ts:40; SplitFormModal:268-276). They are then added with `addShareToSplit` inside `updateSharedSplitForTxn` (slice:98-100), which never calls sendSplitEmail. Only createSharedSplitForTxn emails anyone (72). The 'settled' and 'closed' sends are `void` and their outcome is discarded (119, 133), contrary to the v10.60 'no more silent failures' release note.
- **Failure scenario:** The owner creates a split with Priya, then edits it to add Ravi (no account) and taps 'Yes, send invite'. Ravi gets no email and the owner sees no warning, so Ravi never learns he owes ₹800.
- **Recommendation:** In updateSharedSplitForTxn, collect the newly added share ids and call send-split-email for those shares only. This needs the server's per-share `emailed_at` (see the relay finding). Surface the result the same way the create path does, and apply readSplitEmailResult to settled and closed. Test: a slice unit test that editing to add an email invokes the function once with the new share.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): On edit, new emails get the invite confirmation (SplitFormModal.tsx:269-277; splitParticipants.ts:40), but updateSharedSplitForTxn only calls addShareToSplit, a plain insert (sharedSplitsSlice.ts:98-100; sharedSplits.ts:206-211). No DB trigger sends an email, and only the create path calls sendSplitEmail (:72). The settled and closed sends discard their result (:119, :133).

### BE-PIP-02 · MEDIUM · ask-vyact is a general LLM proxy: client-built system prompts, per-user cap is the only spend control

- **Slice:** ask-vyact gateway and agent service · **Category:** cost · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit A3
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:386-393`, `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:419-440`, `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:474-479`, `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:526-543`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/router.ts:357-396`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/router.ts:193-196`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/assistantCore.ts:49`, `supabase/migrations/20260906140000_seed_model_catalogue.sql:31-32`, `react/src/lib/askVyactModelCall.ts:44-56`
- **Evidence:** The gateway forwards whatever `messages` the browser sends. `sanitiseMessages` allowlists the roles `system|user|assistant|tool` (router.ts:376) and caps them at 40 messages and 24,000 chars (193-196). There is no server-owned prompt template, intent allowlist or output schema. The browser itself builds `{role:'system', content: system}` (askVyactModelCall.ts:50-53). The router comment says roles are allowlisted 'so a client cannot inject a fabricated tool result' (router.ts:361-363), yet 'tool' is on the allowlist. The only spend control is the per-user `reserve_ai_usage` cap (index.ts:419-440): there is no per-household or global budget and no circuit breaker. The cap is `Number.parseInt(env('ASK_VYACT_DAILY_CALL_CAP','200'))`, and both a value of 0 and a mistyped value (NaN fails `Number.isFinite`) skip the reservation entirely, which fails open (index.ts:421, assistantCore.ts:49). The success body returns `provider` and `model` to every caller (535-536), which advertises the backing model. Per ASK_VYACT_STATUS.md, the enabled row was claude-sonnet-5 at $2/$10 per Mtok.
- **Failure scenario:** A script signs up N accounts; no CAPTCHA was found. Each account sends 200 calls a day of 24,000-char arbitrary prompts with its own system message. One call is about 6k input tokens × $2/M = $0.012, plus 600 output tokens × $10/M = $0.006, so about $0.018. That is about $3.60 per account per day; 100 accounts cost about $360/day (~$10.8k/month) on Vyact's OpenRouter key. All of it is legitimately metered as 'ok', and none of it is Pip traffic. Separately, an operator who sets ASK_VYACT_DAILY_CALL_CAP to '200/day' (parses to 200) works, but '' → default and 'unlimited' → NaN silently removes the cap.
- **Recommendation:** Move the prompts server-side. The client sends `{op:'classify'|'phrase', question, payload}`, the gateway composes CLASSIFY_SYSTEM/PHRASE_SYSTEM itself (the bundled engine already carries them for WhatsApp), and it rejects client `system`/`assistant`/`tool` roles. Add a global daily USD budget checked inside `reserve_ai_usage` (sum of cost_usd over 24h across all users), plus a per-household cap. Parse the cap fail-closed: anything non-numeric means 'refuse', and only an explicit 'off' disables it. Drop provider/model from the response. Tests: a gateway handler test showing that a client `system` message gets 400; a reserve test showing the global budget raises 42901; a parse test showing a NaN cap refuses.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The gateway sends client `messages` straight to chatCompletion (index.ts:367,474). sanitiseMessages lets `system` and `tool` through (router.ts:376), which contradicts its own comment at 361-363. The browser builds the system prompt (askVyactModelCall.ts:50-53). The only spend control is the per-user reserve (index.ts:421-440), and a cap that is NaN or 0 skips it entirely. Provider and model are returned at 535-536.…

### BE-PIP-03 · MEDIUM · ai_usage spend gate is client-writable, and client rows eat the gateway quota

- **Slice:** ask-vyact gateway and agent service · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit A2
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260601120000_td06_td21_rls_perf_and_delta_sync_indexes.sql:139-141`, `supabase/migrations/20260815120000_ai_usage_metering.sql:61`, `supabase/migrations/20260909120100_audit_a2_atomic_ai_quota.sql:48-56`, `react/src/lib/aiUsage.ts:84-104`, `react/src/pages/Chat.tsx:254-262`, `react/src/lib/askVyactModelCall.ts:46-56`, `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:272`, `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:395-411`
- **Evidence:** RLS lets any member INSERT into `ai_usage` with only `is_member(household_id) and user_id = auth.uid()` checked (td06:139-141). No column restriction applies, so `cost_usd`, `provider`, `model`, the token counts and `outcome` all come from the client. That contradicts the column comment 'never trusted from a client' (metering:61). `admin_ai_usage_summary` sums `cost_usd` and the tokens across ALL rows. `reserve_ai_usage` counts every row with `backend = 'llm'` in 24h (atomic_quota:48-52), and Chat.tsx writes one client row per question with `backend: assistantBackend?.id ?? 'llm'` (254-262; LlmBackend id is 'llm'). The documented '200 model calls' cap therefore also counts client rows: 2 gateway calls plus 1 client row per question gives about 66 questions/day. Seeded captures, greetings and `meta.assistant` turns make no model call but still burn a slot. The browser never sends `householdId` (askVyactModelCall.ts:46-56), so every web gateway row has `household_id = null` and per-household attribution is impossible. `surface` is client-chosen (index.ts:272).
- **Failure scenario:** (a) Any member runs `supabase.from('ai_usage').insert({household_id: <own>, backend:'llm', cost_usd: 99999})` from devtools, and the admin Intelligence page's cost30Usd reads $99,999: the §9 spend gate is falsified. (b) A household that asks 70 short questions in a day gets 'Daily assistant limit reached' even though the gateway made only about 140 model calls against a cap of 200. (c) Ops cannot answer 'which household is costing us money' because web rows carry no household_id.
- **Recommendation:** Revoke INSERT of the metering columns from `authenticated`, either with column-level privileges or with a BEFORE INSERT trigger that nulls provider/model/tokens/cost_usd/outcome unless the role is service_role. Give gateway rows a distinguishing marker (e.g. `source='gateway'`, or count only rows where `provider is not null`), and count only those in `reserve_ai_usage`. Send `householdId` from `resolveConfiguredModelCall`, and have admin summaries filter to gateway rows. Tests: an RLS SQL test showing a client insert with cost_usd stores NULL; a reserve test showing client rows do not count toward p_cap; a transport unit test asserting householdId is present in the body.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The latest ai_usage INSERT policy checks only is_member plus user_id (td06:140-141). No trigger or column revoke exists in any later migration, so a client can write cost_usd, tokens and outcome. aiUsage.ts:84-104 writes those columns, and Chat.tsx:256 writes backend 'llm' per question. reserve_ai_usage counts every backend='llm' row for the user (atomic_quota:48-52), so client rows use up the cap. The web transport…
- **Also reported as:** DB-SEC-11 (ai_usage metering fields are client-writable, so the LLM spend gate and quota inputs can …)

### BE-WA-03 · MEDIUM · Replies to pending questions are re-parsed as new ledger entries after a retry or a lost pending turn

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:516-551`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:981-1012`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:929-933`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:796-804`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:1117-1131`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:275-310`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:324-352`
- **Evidence:** `processInbound` calls `resolvePending(supabase, pending.id)` (line 518) BEFORE acting on the answer. If the action then fails, it returns `retryOrGiveUp`, which tells the user "that entry is queued — it'll post by itself. Nothing for you to redo" and marks the row failed. Failure points include the reconcile RPC error (1012), the `loadHouseholdRows` failure (995), the name RPC error (933) and the duplicate-check read in logParsed (640). Because the failed RPC rolled back, the message id is unclaimed. On replay `openPending` finds nothing, so the bare text falls through to `parseWhatsAppMessage`. A bare number parses as an expense: `amount` from parseAmount, `category_id` 'other_expense', `account_alias` 'cash'. `askPending` also ignores errors on both its update and its insert (1125-1130; supabase-js does not throw), so a failed insert, or a unique-index race on `uq_wa_pending_open`, has the same effect without any retry.
- **Failure scenario:** The user replies "51300" to "Reply UPDATE · HDFC Savings: what does the bank show now?". The reconcile RPC hits a transient error, and the user is told it is queued. On the next sweep the pending turn is already resolved, so "51300" is logged as a ₹51,300 expense from Cash in Hand (Other), dated the replay day. The HDFC balance is never corrected. Similarly, "1" (yes to a duplicate question) becomes a ₹1 expense, "2 travel" (naming entry 2) becomes a ₹2 Travel expense, and "450" (the missing amount for "groceries hdfc") becomes ₹450 Other from cash instead of Groceries from HDFC.
- **Recommendation:** Resolve the pending turn only after the action commits. Better, resolve it inside the same RPC (pass pending id, or have the RPC mark `whatsapp_pending_turns.resolved_at` in its transaction). Alternatively, store the consumed pending turn's id and payload on the inbox row (`payload.pending`) before acting, and on replay rebuild the context from it instead of re-parsing. Check `error` on askPending/resolvePending and return retry on failure. Add an Edge-handler test: pending balance_update, reconcile RPC fails once, replay; expect the reconcile RPC to be called again and `whatsapp_log_transaction` never called.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): resolvePending runs before the action (webhook:518). A failure in the reconcile RPC (1012), the row load (995), the name RPC (933) or logParsed (640, 668) returns retryOrGiveUp. The RPC rolled back, so the message id is still unclaimed. On the sweep replay (sweepInbox:275), openPending finds nothing, and the parser turns a bare number into an other_expense from cash (parser:324-352). askPending ignores insert errors…

### BE-WA-04 · MEDIUM · Webhook returns 200 to Meta even when the inbox row was not stored, so the message is lost

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:194-218`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:240-246`
- **Evidence:** `await supabase.from('whatsapp_inbound_messages').upsert({... status: 'pending' ...}, { onConflict: 'wa_message_id', ignoreDuplicates: true })`. The `{ error }` is never read, and supabase-js does not throw. The handler always returns `new Response(JSON.stringify({ status: 'ok', recorded: incoming.length }), { status: 200 })`. In `drainInbox` the claim `update ... .eq('status','pending')` then matches 0 rows, and the loop does `continue`. The header comment promises "every message lands as a pending row BEFORE the ACK ... a crash after ACK loses nothing".
- **Failure scenario:** During a short PostgREST or DB outage (connection pool exhausted, failover), a user sends "850 groceries hdfc". The insert fails silently and Meta gets 200, so it never redelivers. No failed row exists for the sweep to replay. The entry is gone, and the user gets no reply because drainInbox skipped it.
- **Recommendation:** Check the upsert error and the `lookupIdentity` error. If any message could not be recorded, return 500 (or 503) so Meta retries the whole delivery. Redelivery is safe because the PK dedups. Add a handler test with a mocked failing insert, expecting a non-2xx response.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:196-206 never reads the upsert's { error } (supabase-js returns errors, it does not throw). Line 216 always returns 200. drainInbox (:243-249) then matches 0 pending rows and `continue`s, and with no row there is nothing for sweepInbox to replay. So one failed insert during a DB blip loses the message with no reply, which contradicts the header comment at :160-167.

### BE-WA-06 · MEDIUM · OTP attempt limit is bypassable by parallel requests and resets on every resend

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-verify-otp/index.ts:68-87`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-send-otp/index.ts:43-61`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp.ts:235-239`
- **Evidence:** verify-otp reads `otp.attempts`, checks `otp.attempts >= MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5), compares the hash, and on mismatch writes `update({ attempts: otp.attempts + 1 })`. This is a read-check-write with no row lock or atomic increment, so N concurrent requests all read 0 and all write 1. send-otp deletes prior codes and inserts a fresh row with attempts 0, rate-limited only per (profile, phone) at 60s. There is no global per-phone or per-IP limit, and the code space is 10^6.
- **Failure scenario:** Once `phone_verification_otp` is approved (today it returns otp_unavailable), an attacker requests a code for the victim's number and fires around 2,000 parallel verify calls in the 10-minute TTL, then repeats each minute. The 5-try cap never binds. Sequentially it still allows 5 guesses per minute per account, times as many accounts as the attacker creates. On success the victim's phone is bound to the attacker's account: the victim's WhatsApp messages (spends, questions) log into the attacker's household, the victim cannot link their own number (`phone_in_use`), and the attacker can push templates to that phone. Each request also sends a paid authentication template to the victim, which is a spam and cost vector.
- **Recommendation:** Make the check atomic: `update whatsapp_verification_otps set attempts = attempts + 1 where id = $1 and attempts < 5 returning attempts, otp_hash` (or an RPC with `for update`), and compare only if a row came back. Count attempts per (phone_number) across resends, e.g. lock out after 10 failures per 24h, and add a per-phone send limit across all profiles. Test: 20 concurrent wrong codes leave attempts == 5 and all later calls return too_many_attempts.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp-verify-otp/index.ts:68-87 reads attempts, checks >=5, then writes attempts+1 non-atomically, so parallel guesses all pass the cap. send-otp/index.ts:43-61 deletes the old code and inserts a fresh one with attempts 0 every 60s. There is no per-phone cap across profiles. It is not exploitable today because the OTP template is unapproved (otp_unavailable at :76-80), so medium.

### BE-WA-07 · MEDIUM · whatsapp-notify lets any member send any template, with arbitrary text and button payloads, from Vyact's number

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-notify/index.ts:48-73`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp.ts:116-136`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-send.ts:132-143`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:405-425`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-prefs.ts:58`
- **Evidence:** With a member JWT holding a write role, the body `{ event, householdId, toProfileId, params, dedupeKey }` is passed straight to `guardedSend`. `params` are free strings (cleanParam only strips newlines and caps at 200 chars), and `dedupeKey` becomes the quick-reply payload context (`${def.name}:${index}:${context}`). Every template in the manifest is reachable, including `bill_overdue_reminder` (context `bill:<schedule>:<date>`, whose "Already paid" approves that schedule), `recurring_auto_logged` (`rec:<txnId>`, whose "Undo" soft-deletes that txn) and `affordability_reply` (topic 'answers', consent 'service', so it is sent even when the recipient never turned on reads). CLAUDE.md requires that scheduler figures come from the money port only. This path lets a client supply the figures.
- **Failure scenario:** A household member, or a former member's account still in the household, calls whatsapp-notify with event `large_transaction` and params ["18,000", "HDFC card. Call 98xxxx to block"]. The partner receives a Vyact-branded alert with a fake phone number. Or the member sends `bill_overdue_reminder` showing "₹10" with dedupeKey `bill:<rentScheduleId>:2026-10-01`; the partner taps "Already paid" and posts the real ₹25,000 rent under their own name. A new dedupeKey per call also defeats dedupe; only the daily cap of 6 limits volume.
- **Recommendation:** Restrict member-initiated notify to an allowlist of events that make sense from a client (for example partner_split_prompt), and compute their values server-side from ids (txn id, then amount and description from the DB), not from `params`. Ignore a client `dedupeKey` for templates whose payload context drives a write (bill_*, recurring_auto_logged, partner_split_prompt). Require reads consent for `affordability_reply`. Test: notify with a member JWT and event bill_overdue_reminder returns 403.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp-notify/index.ts:48-73 accepts any template event, free params and dedupeKey from any write-role member. guardedSend (whatsapp-send.ts:134-143) uses dedupeKey as the slot and the quick-reply context. A forged `bill:<id>:<date>` becomes a real Already-paid approval (webhook :420-425) and matches the 'paid X' lookup. The recipient must be linked to the same household (:93-97), so this is insider-only. 'Former …

### BE-WA-08 · MEDIUM · "No, that was X" can give an entry the wrong category type, and allows Loan/EMI

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:845-860`, `supabase/migrations/20260928120000_w3_whatsapp_capture_conversation.sql:130-155`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-conversation.ts:50-63`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-followups.ts:1-8`
- **Evidence:** `correctLast` decides type-fit from the most recent inbound row of any kind: `.eq('direction','inbound').eq('profile_id',...).neq('wa_message_id', message.id).order('processed_at', { ascending: false }).limit(1)`. PostgREST's `.desc` is NULLS FIRST, so any row with a null processed_at wins: failed or pending rows, which keep processed_at null forever once retries are exhausted. A row that is not the logged entry ("hi", "menu") has no `parsed`. Either way `type` is '' and `if (type && !categoryFitsType(...))` is skipped. The RPC then picks the last LOGGED entry and does `update public.transactions set category = p_category` with no type check (comment: "the caller checked"). `parseCorrection` can return income ids (salary, gift_bonus) and `loan_emi`. The W6 naming rule (followups.ts:4-7) forbids relabelling a plain expense as Loan / EMI, but the correction path permits it.
- **Failure scenario:** The user logs "2000 dinner", sends "thanks", then "no, that was bonus" within 15 minutes. The expense is stored with category `gift_bonus`. It drops out of needs/wants (no NEEDS_WANTS_MAP entry for an income id under expense) and shows as an unknown category in spend breakdowns. Alternatively, "actually emi" turns a ₹15,000 shopping expense into an unlinked `loan_emi`, so the debt's figures never see the principal.
- **Recommendation:** Move the validation into `whatsapp_correct_last`. After locking the txn, check `p_category` against the type's allowlist (a SQL array kept in parity with constants.ts, as the CON-UNIT-161/162 tests do), and reject `loan_emi` with status `approve_in_app`. In the webhook, read the type from the RPC result (or from `whatsapp_last_logged`) instead of guessing from the latest inbox row. Test: income txn plus correction to 'groceries' returns not_allowed; expense plus correction to 'loan_emi' returns approve_in_app.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): correctLast (index.ts:850-853) guesses the type from the latest inbound row via order(processed_at desc), with Postgres default NULLS FIRST. A 'thanks' row has no parsed, so type is '' and the check is skipped. whatsapp_correct_last (w3 migration :143-147) updates category with no type check. ck_txn_category_by_type checks only NOT NULL. loan_emi is in EXPENSE_IDS (parser.ts:19), so 'actually emi' passes even the no…

### BE-WA-09 · MEDIUM · STOP and other consent/state writes ignore Supabase errors yet reply "Done"

- **Slice:** WhatsApp integration (webhook, OTP, notify, dispatch) · **Category:** compliance · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:106-121`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:481-487`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:95-103`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:256-266`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-send.ts:144-152`
- **Evidence:** `savePrefs` does `await supabase.from('whatsapp_preferences').upsert({...})` and never reads `{ error }`. The caller wraps it in `try { await savePrefs(...) } catch (e) { return { status: 'retry' ... } }`, but supabase-js resolves with `{ error }` rather than throwing, so the catch is dead code. The reply "Done. I've stopped everything I can stop here" is then sent. The same pattern applies to `recordMetaOptOut` (Meta's marketing stop), the done/failed status updates in `drainInbox`, and guardedSend's final `status: 'sent'` update. CLAUDE.md: "never a silent write-loss catch {} on a write/contract path."
- **Failure scenario:** A user sends STOP during a transient DB error. They are told everything is stopped, but `muted_topics` and `marketing_opt_in` are unchanged, so tomorrow's digest and weekly marketing still arrive. That is a consent breach the user was explicitly told would not happen. If drainInbox's 'done' update fails, the row stays 'claimed' and the sweep replays it after 10 minutes, re-sending replies. If guardedSend's 'sent' update fails, the row stays 'sending': it is not counted against the daily cap, and "paid Rent" cannot find the reminder (it filters `status='sent'`).
- **Recommendation:** Destructure and check `error` on every write in the webhook and in guardedSend. For savePrefs and recordMetaOptOut, return `{status:'retry'}` and do not send the confirmation until the write succeeds. Add a small helper `must(await q)` that throws on `error`. Handler test: a failing upsert on STOP produces a 'retry' outcome and no "Done" reply.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): savePrefs (index.ts:106-120) and recordMetaOptOut (:95-102) await upserts without reading { error }, so the try/catch at :483-484 and :420 never sees a PostgREST failure and the STOP 'Done' reply goes out. The drainInbox done/failed updates (:256-266) and guardedSend's final 'sent' update (whatsapp-send.ts:144-147) are also unchecked. The trigger needs a transient DB error, so medium.

## Database

### DB-SEC-01 · CRITICAL · replace_* bulk RPCs let any member, even a viewer or child, take over or wipe a household

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** critical · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** TD-42
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260529151000_td09_replace_all_rpcs.sql:128-146`, `supabase/migrations/20260529151000_td09_replace_all_rpcs.sql:20-38`, `supabase/migrations/20260529151000_td09_replace_all_rpcs.sql:83-122`, `supabase/migrations/20260529151500_td08_td09_harden_execute_grants.sql:29-34`, `supabase/migrations/20260614130000_v931_budget_identity_convergence.sql:42-91`, `supabase/migrations/20260602130000_money_map_replace_accounts_rpc.sql:13-44`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:1238-1249`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:208-222`
- **Evidence:** Every replace_<entity>(h, rows) is SECURITY DEFINER, so it bypasses RLS. Its only gate is `if not is_member(h)`, and is_member is true for every role: owner, admin, member, viewer and child. replace_memberships runs `delete from memberships where household_id = h;` and then inserts caller-supplied rows `(id, household_id, user_id, role, ...)` from `jsonb_populate_recordset`, with no limit on role. replace_transactions, replace_debts, replace_assets, replace_accounts and replace_budgets each start with `update <table> set deleted_at = now() where household_id = h`. EXECUTE is still granted to authenticated (only public and anon were revoked). The S2 role rules and the v9.5.0 owner/admin budget rule apply only through RLS or upsert_budget, and these functions sidestep both. The only app caller is the local-mode demo seed (`!cloudEnabled ... active === 'local'`), so in cloud mode these RPCs are reachable only by direct /rest/v1/rpc calls.
- **Failure scenario:** A household has an owner, a partner (admin) and a teenager (child). The teenager signs in and POSTs /rest/v1/rpc/replace_memberships {h: <household>, rows: [{id: <new uuid>, user_id: <own uid>, role: 'owner', display_name: 'x', joined_at: now}]}. Both adult memberships are deleted and the teenager is now the only owner. The adults lose all access, and the teenager can then DELETE the household, which cascades through every financial row. Second scenario: a viewer calls replace_transactions(h, '[]') and all 1,240 transactions are soft-deleted in one statement. Cash Flow and Net Worth for every month drop to the opening balances, and the app cannot undo it.
- **Recommendation:** Drop replace_transactions, replace_budgets, replace_goals, replace_debts, replace_assets, replace_memberships and replace_accounts (and replace_saved_views) in a new migration, and route the local-mode seed through the local adapter only. If a cloud restore path is ever needed, rebuild it as an owner-only RPC (`role_in(h) = 'owner'`) that never touches memberships and validates every row. Proving test: a PGlite or pgTAP case that sets request.jwt.claims to a viewer and a child and asserts each replace_* call raises 42501, or no longer exists, leaving memberships and transactions unchanged.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): td09_replace_all_rpcs.sql:128-143: SECURITY DEFINER replace_memberships deletes every membership, then inserts caller rows with any role. Its only gate is is_member(h) (baseline:366, true for every role). EXECUTE stays granted to authenticated (td08_td09:29-34 revokes public/anon only). No later migration drops these functions or adds FORCE RLS. The member-limit trigger (household_types_v2:83) sees 0 rows after the …
- **Also reported as:** DB-INT-01 (replace_* bulk RPCs let any member (viewer/child) wipe the ledger and rewrite memberships); DB-OPS-01 (replace_memberships/replace_* RPCs let any viewer or child seize a household or wipe its …); INT-CON-02 (replace_memberships RPC lets any household member rewrite all memberships and self-promot…)

### DB-SEC-02 · CRITICAL · transfer_ownership's owner check passes for non-members (NULL <> 'owner'), letting anyone promote members

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** critical · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S2
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:473-482`, `supabase/migrations/20260909150000_v1022_lock_down_definer_functions.sql:58-69`, `supabase/migrations/20260909150000_v1022_lock_down_definer_functions.sql:96`, `supabase/migrations/20260601120000_td06_td21_rls_perf_and_delta_sync_indexes.sql:92-95`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:131-134`
- **Evidence:** Latest body (baseline, with only search_path pinned in v10.22): `if (select role from memberships where household_id = h_id and user_id = auth.uid()) <> 'owner' then raise exception 'only_owner_can_transfer'; end if; update memberships set role = 'owner' where household_id = h_id and user_id = to_user; update memberships set role = 'admin' where household_id = h_id and user_id = auth.uid();`. When the caller has no membership in h_id, the scalar subquery returns NULL. `NULL <> 'owner'` is NULL, and PL/pgSQL treats an IF on NULL as false, so no exception is raised. The function is SECURITY DEFINER and is granted to authenticated. It also never checks that to_user is a member, and the memberships DELETE policy lets the last owner delete their own row directly, which skips leave_household's last_owner check.
- **Failure scenario:** A viewer V in household H registers a free second account X that is not a member of H. X calls rpc('transfer_ownership', {h_id: H, to_user: V}). The guard evaluates NULL and passes, and V's row becomes role='owner'. V now has full owner rights: delete the household, change base_currency (which relabels every account), remove the real owner. An outsider who knows a household id and a member's user id can do the same; split participants see both through shared_splits.owner_household_id and owner_user_id. Separately, a real owner who calls it with a to_user who is not a member updates 0 rows and then demotes themself to admin, leaving the household with no owner.
- **Recommendation:** Rewrite transfer_ownership as: `if auth.uid() is null or coalesce(role_in(h_id), '') <> 'owner' then raise ... using errcode='42501'`. Lock the target row and require it to exist (`if not found then raise 'not_a_member'`), and run both updates only after that. Apply the same coalesce pattern to every role comparison in a SECURITY DEFINER function. Enforce owner continuity in a trigger on memberships (block a DELETE or UPDATE that leaves a household with zero owners). Tests: a non-member caller gets 42501 and the target's role is unchanged; a to_user who is not a member is refused and the caller stays owner; deleting the last owner's own membership is refused.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): baseline:473-481 is the only body; v1022:58,96 only re-grants and pins search_path. For a non-member, the scalar subquery is NULL, so `NULL <> 'owner'` is NULL and the IF never raises. The UPDATE runs as definer and bypasses the S2 RLS (audit_s2:30). to_user is never checked. A viewer's second account, or anyone who knows the ids, can promote any member to owner. Owner continuity is not enforced (td06:92-95).
- **Also reported as:** DB-INT-02 (transfer_ownership: NULL comparison lets a non-member make any member an owner); INT-CON-01 (transfer_ownership NULL guard lets a non-owner / non-member grant ownership); INT-HH-01 (transfer_ownership NULL-comparison bypass lets any non-member make a household member own…)

### DB-INT-05 · HIGH · erase_household_data always rolls back: its own audit insert violates the activity_log CHECK

- **Slice:** Constraints, RPC atomicity and money correctness in SQL · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S6 (the onboarding cause is fixed; this second cause is still open)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260911150000_v1030_net_worth_snapshots.sql:119-156`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:194`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:212-215`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:159`

(153-154). activity_log.action has 'check (action in ('created','updated','deleted','invited','accepted','removed','role_changed','transferred'))' (baseline:194), and no migration alters it (grep finds only the baseline definition). The function is one transaction, so the 23514 error rolls back every preceding delete. The S6 fix replaced the onboarding NULL but left this second schema violation in place. Separately, a successful erase would still leave shared_splits (owner_household_id), notifications, whatsapp_inbound_messages payloads, whatsapp_pending_turns and exchange_rates untouched.
