The function deletes the transactions, budgets and so on, raises 'new row for relation activity_log violates check constraint' on its last statement, and everything is restored. The user sees a generic error and every transaction remains. 'Erase my data' cannot succeed in any environment built from these migrations.
- **Recommendation:** Use an allowed action value such as 'deleted' with changes->>'kind' = 'erase', or widen the CHECK in the same migration. Also delete or anonymise shared_splits owned by the household, notifications, WhatsApp payloads and pending turns. Add an integration test that runs erase_household_data inside a DO block that ends in RAISE, asserting it reaches the end and that transaction counts reach 0.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The latest erase_household_data (v1030:119-156) ends by inserting action='erase_household_data'. The activity_log CHECK in baseline:194 allows only created, updated, deleted, invited, accepted, removed, role_changed and transferred, and no migration alters or drops it. So the whole function rolls back on every call. The S6 fix changed only the onboarding NULL. No SQL test covers erase. Settings.tsx:159/866 reaches i…
- **Also reported as:** DB-OPS-04 (erase_household_data always rolls back: its audit insert violates activity_log's action C…)

### DB-SEC-03 · HIGH · upsert_budget/replace_budgets ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE has no household guard: cross-household budget overwrite and read

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260614140000_v933_upsert_budget_identity_authority.sql:90-96`, `supabase/migrations/20260614140000_v933_upsert_budget_identity_authority.sql:63-74`, `supabase/migrations/20260614130000_v931_budget_identity_convergence.sql:57-88`, `supabase/migrations/20260621120000_upsert_budget_with_allocations.sql:26`
- **Evidence:** In 'replace' mode, a budget whose scope is neither 'month' nor 'annual' (scope NULL is allowed by ck_budget_scope) takes the else-branch: `insert into budgets (id, household_id, ...) values (v.id, h, ...) on conflict (id) do update set category=excluded.category, monthly_limit=excluded.monthly_limit, ..., deleted_at=null returning * into rec; return next rec;`. v.id comes from the caller's JSON. The DO UPDATE has no `where budgets.household_id = h`, and the function runs as definer, so RLS does not apply. The caller only has to pass is_member(h) for their own household h; in production they would also need to be owner/admin of it, which anyone is for a household they create. replace_budgets has the same unguarded `on conflict (id) do update set ... deleted_at = excluded.deleted_at` and returns the updated row.
- **Failure scenario:** A partner is removed from household H, but their browser cache still holds H's budget ids. They create their own household H2 (and become its owner) and call rpc('upsert_budget', {h: H2, b: {id: <H's October budget id>, monthly_limit: 1, period: 'monthly'}, p_mode: 'replace'}). The PK conflict updates H's row: monthly_limit goes from 60,000 to 1, so every allocation reads as over budget, and a deleted budget is revived. The full row (household_id, category, amounts) is returned to the attacker. replace_budgets(H2, [{id: <H budget id>, deleted_at: now()}]) soft-deletes it instead.
- **Recommendation:** In every SECURITY DEFINER upsert, constrain the conflict update to the caller's tenant: `on conflict (id) do update set ... where budgets.household_id = h`. Treat a NULL `rec` afterwards as BUDGET_EXISTS or 42501. Better still, ignore client-supplied ids in upsert_budget (the v9.3.3 header already says 'The DB assigns the id; the client never sends one'). Drop replace_budgets (see the replace_* finding). Test: as the owner of H2, call upsert_budget with a budget id from H and assert an exception, no change to H's row, and no row returned.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): v933:90-95: in replace mode, a NULL scope takes the else-branch, `on conflict (id) do update` has no household_id predicate, and the row is returned. ck_budget_scope allows NULL scope (20260613120000:18). Create mode is safe (DO NOTHING raises BUDGET_EXISTS); the month and annual arbiters raise a PK violation. v931:67-88 replace_budgets has the same gap and can also set deleted_at. upsert_budget_with_allocations the…
- **Also reported as:** DB-INT-03 (upsert_budget/replace_budgets ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE overwrites other households' bud…); DB-OPS-02 (upsert_budget/replace_budgets ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE overwrites other households' bud…)

### DB-INT-09 · MEDIUM · Account and asset tombstones are not enforced: in-use and cash accounts can be deleted, and live rows land on deleted ones

- **Slice:** Constraints, RPC atomicity and money correctness in SQL · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260910150000_r2_accounts_card_metadata_delete_guard.sql:139-178`, `supabase/migrations/20260602120000_money_map_phase1_accounts.sql:134-136`, `supabase/migrations/20260909120000_audit_s3_tenant_consistency.sql:24-59`, `supabase/migrations/20260910120000_r1_accounts_one_cash_household_currency.sql:249-256`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:258-262`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:981`
- **Evidence:** delete_account locks the account row, counts dependencies with account_dependencies, and tombstones it (r2:149-176). Nothing stops a transaction being written against a tombstoned account later: the tenant trigger checks only 'id = new.account_id and household_id = new.household_id' (s3:30-36), not deleted_at. The 'accounts_update' RLS policy lets any owner, admin or member UPDATE an account (money_map:134-136), so 'set deleted_at = now()' or 'is_archived = true' on Cash in Hand or an in-use account skips delete_account's cash_account_protected and account_in_use checks. ensure_cash_account returns an archived cash row (r1:250-252, no is_archived filter), and its unique index blocks a replacement, while whatsapp_log_transaction ignores archived cash (r4:258-262). The client drops tombstoned accounts from every list (supabaseAdapter.ts:981). The asset side has the same gap (assets_refuse_delete_in_use checks only at tombstone time).
- **Failure scenario:** Phone A, offline, logs a ₹5,000 expense on HDFC; it is queued. Phone B deletes HDFC, which has no dependencies on the server yet. Phone A reconnects and upserts the expense, which the server accepts. The expense counts in cash flow, but it debits no visible account, so Net Worth is overstated by ₹5,000. Separately, a member who archives Cash in Hand through PostgREST leaves WhatsApp logging failing with no_source_account, and the household cannot get a new cash account.
- **Recommendation:** Add a BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger on transactions that locks the referenced accounts and asset with 'select ... for share' and refuses deleted_at IS NOT NULL. A queued write then fails into dead-letter where it is visible, rather than silently landing. Add a BEFORE UPDATE trigger on accounts that refuses deleted_at or is_archived on kind='cash', and refuses deleted_at unless set from delete_account or move_account_and_delete (session flag or role). Tests: insert a transaction on a tombstoned account (expect an error), archive cash through PostgREST (expect an error).
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The tenant trigger (s3:30-43) checks only the household, not deleted_at, and no other insert/update trigger exists on transactions. The accounts_update RLS policy (money_map:134-136) lets members UPDATE deleted_at or is_archived directly, which skips delete_account's cash and in-use checks (r2:159-172). ensure_cash_account omits is_archived (r1:249-251) while the WhatsApp RPC filters archived cash out (r4:258-262).
- **Also reported as:** DB-SEC-05 (The account delete guard is RPC-only: RLS lets any member soft-delete or archive Cash and…); INT-TXN-09 (Transactions can land on a deleted (tombstoned) account after delete/move; money vanishes)

### DB-INT-11 · MEDIUM · No unique (household_id, user_id) on memberships; invite accept and member limit race

- **Slice:** Constraints, RPC atomicity and money correctness in SQL · **Category:** concurrency · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:69-78`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:370-372`, `supabase/migrations/20260601153000_fix_shareable_invite_accept_rpc.sql:29-54`, `supabase/migrations/20261007120000_household_types_v2.sql:52-65`
- **Evidence:** memberships has no unique constraint on (household_id, user_id); grep finds none in any migration or in db/schema.sql. accept_invitation_link checks for an existing membership and then inserts (29-52) with no lock. enforce_household_member_limit counts 'select count(*) from memberships where household_id = new.household_id' (58) without locking the household row, so concurrent inserts each see the old count. role_in reads 'select role from memberships where ... limit 1' with no ORDER BY (baseline:372), so duplicate rows give a nondeterministic role.
- **Failure scenario:** The invitee double-taps Accept (or two tabs submit). Both transactions see no membership and a count of 1, and both insert. A Couple household now holds 3 membership rows, past its limit of 2. If the owner later demotes one row to viewer, role_in returns 'member' or 'viewer' depending on heap order, so the demotion only sometimes holds.
- **Recommendation:** De-duplicate the existing rows, then add 'create unique index uq_membership_user on memberships(household_id, user_id) where user_id is not null'. Use 'insert ... on conflict do nothing' in accept_invitation_link. In the limit trigger, take 'select ... from households where id = new.household_id for update' before counting. Test with two concurrent accept calls.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The baseline only creates non-unique indexes on memberships (346-347); there is no unique (household_id, user_id). accept_invitation_link (20260601153000:19-52) reads the invite and the membership without FOR UPDATE, and its accepted_at update does not re-check the value. The limit trigger (v2:58) counts without locking the household row. role_in uses limit 1 with no ORDER BY (baseline:372). Concurrent accepts can e…
- **Also reported as:** DB-OPS-15 (Household member-limit trigger is race-prone (count without lock))

### DB-INT-13 · MEDIUM · WhatsApp account alias matches by kind with 'limit 1', booking money to an arbitrary account

- **Slice:** Constraints, RPC atomicity and money correctness in SQL · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:264-271`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:288-295`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:262-270`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:340-352`
- **Evidence:** The parser turns the words 'bank', 'card' and 'credit card' into the aliases 'bank' and 'credit_card' (parser:262-265) and passes them as p_account_alias. The RPC resolves an alias with 'where household_id = ... and (lower(name) = lower(p_account_alias) or lower(kind) = lower(p_account_alias)) limit 1' with no ORDER BY (r4:265-271, 289-295), so with two bank accounts Postgres returns whichever row the heap gives first. A 'loan' kind account can also match.
- **Failure scenario:** A household has HDFC (bank) and ICICI (bank). The message '2000 rent from bank' debits whichever row comes first, today ICICI and after a VACUUM or update HDFC. Each account balance is off by ₹2,000 until a reconcile writes a spurious offset. Net Worth is unchanged, but account balances and 'spend by account' are wrong.
- **Recommendation:** When a kind alias matches more than one live account, return 'ambiguous_account' and ask the user, or prefer the default account deterministically: 'order by is_default desc, created_at, id'. Never resolve to kind 'loan'. Add RPC tests with two accounts of the same kind.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The parser maps 'bank', 'card' and 'credit card' to kind aliases (whatsapp-parser.ts:262-265). The latest RPC (r4:264-271, 288-295) matches the name or kind with limit 1 and no ORDER BY, so two bank accounts give an arbitrary pick. The 'loan' part is overstated: the parser never emits 'loan', so it matches only an account literally named loan. Blast radius is limited to linked WhatsApp users.

### DB-OPS-03 · MEDIUM · Repo no longer reproduces prod: budget owner/admin guard and other objects exist only in prod

- **Slice:** Schema evolution, performance and operations · **Category:** architecture · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** TD-20
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260617120000_v950_budgets_owner_admin_only_plus_realtime.sql:29-36`, `supabase/migrations/20260614140000_v933_upsert_budget_identity_authority.sql:26-41`, `supabase/migrations/20260621120000_upsert_budget_with_allocations.sql:9-26`, `supabase/migrations/20260608120000_v9_txn_redesign.sql:4-6`, `supabase/migrations/20260608120000_v9_txn_redesign.sql:27-33`, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:39-40`, `TECH_DEBT.md:1279-1282`
- **Evidence:** v950 contains no SQL for the owner/admin guard on upsert_budget. It has only a comment: "(Applied to prod via CREATE OR REPLACE with the full body.)". The last definition in the migrations (v933:26-41) checks only `is_member(h)`, while upsert_budget_with_allocations claims to 'Reuse upsert_budget for ... the owner/admin guard'. Other objects also differ from the repo. The `_backup_v9_*` tables are production-only (ENVIRONMENTS.md). TD-42 says migration_issues has RLS enabled in production, but no migration ever enables RLS on it (v9:27-33 creates it in public with no RLS). my_households was absent from the 'faithful' baseline and first appears as `drop view if exists` + create in v8. This is the parallel-history failure that TD-20 was closed to prevent.
- **Failure scenario:** vyact-test, or any environment rebuilt from the migrations for a restore or a new region, lets a plain `member` create and replace budgets, so Lane B validates different authorization than production. Separately, the next developer who copies the 'canonical body' from v933 into a CREATE OR REPLACE silently removes the production-only guard, and no gate notices. On a migrations-built database, migration_issues sits in public with Supabase's default anon/authenticated grants and no RLS.
- **Recommendation:** Run `select pg_get_functiondef('public.upsert_budget(uuid,jsonb,text)'::regprocedure)` on production and commit it verbatim as a new migration that includes the guard. Add a migration that enables RLS on migration_issues and drops or archives the `_backup_v9_*` tables. Add a CI drift check that diffs `supabase db dump --schema-only` from production (or a schema hash) against a fresh database built from the migrations, at least weekly.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): v950:29-36 has the owner/admin guard only as a comment. The only upsert_budget definition is v933:26-41, with is_member alone. migration_issues is created in v9:27-33, v91 and v91_feedback with no RLS anywhere, yet TD-42 (TECH_DEBT.md:1280) says production has RLS on it. Production itself has the guard, and ENVIRONMENTS.md claims vyact-test parity, so this is a structural weakness: medium.
- **Also reported as:** DB-SEC-04 (Migrations are not the source of truth: the upsert_budget owner/admin guard exists only i…); DB-INT-04 (upsert_budget owner/admin guard exists only as a comment, not in any migration)

### DB-OPS-08 · MEDIUM · Scheduler runs are unobservable, and web-push re-reads every subscribed ledger every 15 minutes

- **Slice:** Schema evolution, performance and operations · **Category:** scalability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:210-217`, `supabase/functions/web-push/index.ts:120-176`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/householdLoader.ts:16-39`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-dispatch/index.ts:64-108`
- **Evidence:** Cron jobs only enqueue `net.http_post(... timeout_milliseconds := 55000)`, so cron.job_run_details records success whatever the function returns. Responses go to net._http_response, which pg_net keeps for about 6 hours and nothing reads. The dispatchers return `problems` and tallies only in that response body. web-push dispatch loops sequentially over every household that has an active subscription and calls loadHouseholdRows. That drains ALL live transactions, budgets, allocations, accounts and schedules with offset pagination ordered by id (`.order('id').range(from, from+PAGE-1)`), plus every notification dedupe_key for the household, every 15 minutes, before stage 2 pushes anything. No alerting or metric is attached to either job.
- **Failure scenario:** At a few hundred subscribed households with multi-year ledgers, stage 1 exceeds the Edge wall-clock limit, the invocation is killed, and stage 2 (push) never runs for anyone. No P1 push reaches any device, cron reports success, and the only evidence expires from net._http_response within hours. The same silent-success shape applies to a WhatsApp digest that fails for one household.
- **Recommendation:** Record each run in a dispatch_runs table (job, started_at, finished_at, planned, sent, problems) written by the function itself, and alert when a 15-minute job has no successful row in 45 minutes. Make the web-push raise step incremental: rows changed since the last run (updated_at cursor), or move notification generation to write time. Push before raising, or in a separate job. Use keyset pagination on (household_id, id) for the loader.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Confirmed: web-push/index.ts:120-176 drains a full ledger per subscribed household sequentially (householdLoader.ts:16-39, offset pagination) before any push. `problems` exists only in the response body, and pg_cron sees just the net.http_post enqueue. Partly mitigated: push_deliveries (index.ts:100-110) records each send outcome. The 'few hundred households' timeout is extrapolated, not shown.

### DB-OPS-10 · MEDIUM · Delta-sync cursor relies on updated_at = now(), so out-of-order commits are skipped forever

- **Slice:** Schema evolution, performance and operations · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit F8
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:358-360`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:1037-1063`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:121-127`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:227`
- **Evidence:** set_updated_at() assigns `new.updated_at = now()`, which is the transaction START time, not commit time, and inserts default to now(). listSince requests rows strictly after the stored (updated_at, id) cursor (`updated_at.gt.${sinceTs},and(updated_at.eq...,id.gt...)`), and hybridAdapter writes `delta.maxUpdatedAt` back as the new cursor. Nothing re-reads an overlap window. Audit F8 fixed tie stalls but not commit ordering.
- **Failure scenario:** Transaction A (an RPC such as record_loan_payment or whatsapp_log_transaction) starts at 10:00:00.100 and commits at 10:00:00.400. Transaction B, a partner's expense, starts at 10:00:00.200 and commits at 10:00:00.250. A device polls at 10:00:00.300, sees B, and advances its cursor to .200. A is now behind the cursor and is never fetched, so that device permanently omits, say, a ₹15,000 EMI from Cash Flow and balances until someone runs forceFullResync.
- **Recommendation:** Re-read with an overlap window (cursor minus about 30 s; upserts are idempotent), or add a commit-ordered column (for example, assign `pg_current_xact_id()` and only advance the cursor past `pg_snapshot_xmin(pg_current_snapshot())`). Add a concurrency test with two sessions that commit out of start order and assert the reader eventually sees both rows.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): set_updated_at uses now(), the transaction start time (baseline:358-360). listSince filters strictly after the (updated_at,id) cursor (supabaseAdapter.ts:1046-1050), and applyCloudDelta advances the cursor with no overlap (hybridAdapter.ts:227). seedCursorFromRows has the same race. The only full re-read is forceFullResync, a user action, or a cache purge. The race window is narrow (milliseconds of transaction overl…

### DB-OPS-12 · MEDIUM · Documented pg_dump --no-privileges restore strips every REVOKE the security model depends on

- **Slice:** Schema evolution, performance and operations · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** TD-16
- **Where:** `OPERATIONS.md:57-112`, `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:134-142`, `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:188-195`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:385-386`, `supabase/migrations/20260909120100_audit_a2_atomic_ai_quota.sql:69-70`, `supabase/migrations/20260909150000_v1022_lock_down_definer_functions.sql:36-72`
- **Evidence:** The runbook's backup and restore commands use `--no-owner --no-privileges` for both pg_dump and pg_restore. --no-privileges omits all GRANT/REVOKE statements, and recreated functions fall back to the default PUBLIC EXECUTE. Several service-only functions have no internal caller check and rely entirely on `revoke all ... from public, anon, authenticated`. web_push_vapid() returns the VAPID private key from Vault. whatsapp_log_transaction(p_profile_id, p_household_id, ...) writes as any profile. reserve_ai_usage and whatsapp_dispatch_ok are in the same position. The repo contains no PITR or managed-backup posture; the v9 header notes 'PITR not triggerable via MCP'.
- **Failure scenario:** After an incident, an operator follows OPERATIONS.md §4 to restore a dump. On the restored database, any anon or signed-in client can call /rest/v1/rpc/web_push_vapid and read the private key, or call whatsapp_log_transaction to post transactions into any household by guessing a profile and household pair. The tables that had `revoke all from authenticated` also regain Supabase's default grants.
- **Recommendation:** Remove --no-privileges from the runbook, or follow every restore with a re-application of a committed `grants.sql`. Add internal guards (`if current_user <> 'service_role' then raise`) to the service-only definer functions so they do not depend on ACLs alone. Document which Supabase plan, PITR window and backup frequency production actually has, and rehearse a restore into vyact-test, asserting `has_function_privilege('anon', ...)` is false for every SECURITY DEFINER function.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): OPERATIONS.md §3 and §4 both use --no-owner --no-privileges, so a restore drops every REVOKE. web_push_vapid (web_push.sql:134-142,188) and reserve_ai_usage rely only on ACLs. Mitigations: §4 targets staging or disposable databases only; whatsapp_log_transaction has an internal membership gate (r4:~436), so it is not 'any household'; and Vault ciphertext likely won't decrypt on another project. Exposure is still rea…

### DB-SEC-06 · MEDIUM · Any member, even a viewer, can insert P1 notifications that web-push sends to every member's lock screen

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260709120000_v101_notifications_sync.sql:44-49`, `supabase/functions/web-push/index.ts:153-176`, `supabase/functions/_shared/web-push-rules.ts:74-94`, `react/src/lib/notificationsCloud.ts:16-44`
- **Evidence:** Policies: `notif_insert ... with check (is_member(household_id))`, and `notif_update`/`notif_delete using (is_member(household_id))` with no role restriction and no check on priority, type, title or body. The dispatcher selects every household notification with `pushed_at is null` created in the window and sends `payloadFor(n)`, which is `{ title: n.title, body: n.body, ... }`, to every member's device when `priority === 'P1'` and status is unread. Nothing distinguishes server-raised rows (notificationsOnServer) from client-inserted ones.
- **Failure scenario:** A viewer inserts {household_id: H, type: 'bill_due', priority: 'P1', status: 'unread', title: 'Vyact security alert', body: 'Your account is locked, call +91-98xxxxxx to restore', dedupe_key: random}. Within 15 minutes every other member's phone shows it as a genuine Vyact notification. With fresh dedupe_keys this can repeat every cycle without limit. The same viewer can also delete or mark read the real 'EMI due tomorrow' P1 for everyone, which suppresses the push (skipReason 'already_seen').
- **Recommendation:** Push only notifications the server raised: add `source text not null default 'client'`, set 'server' in web-push's insert, and filter `source = 'server'` in the pending query, or move P1 generation entirely server-side. Add a WITH CHECK limiting client inserts to priority 'P2' and to a type allowlist, and restrict update and delete to non-viewer roles. Test: a viewer inserting a P1 row is either rejected or never selected by dispatch().
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): notif_insert, notif_update and notif_delete (v101_notifications_sync.sql:45-47) check only is_member, so a viewer can write, and the table CHECK allows priority P1. web-push/index.ts:153-170 selects every unpushed row in the window and sends payloadFor(n) title/body. skipReason (web-push-rules.ts:77-91) never tells server-raised rows from client-inserted ones. Impact stays inside one household.
- **Also reported as:** FE-PLT-05 (Any household member can push a spoofed lock-screen notification that opens an external s…)

### DB-SEC-08 · MEDIUM · erase_household_data: email-code gate is client-only, and the erase misses message text and notifications

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S6
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260911150000_v1030_net_worth_snapshots.sql:119-161`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:152-153`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:153-159`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:212-215`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:263-284`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:196-205`
- **Evidence:** Privacy promises that every Danger Zone action 'requires entering a one-time verification code ... proof the request really is coming from you, not just a signed-in browser'. However, verifyAccountActionCode only calls auth.verifyOtp in the browser, and erase_household_data(h_id) checks only `caller_role in ('owner','admin')`. It has no aal/amr/iat recency check, so any valid session (and any admin) can call it directly. The latest body deletes transactions, budgets, allocations, goals, debts, assets, accounts, recurring_schedules, saved_views, net_worth_snapshots and activity_log. It leaves notifications (titles, bodies, amount_ref), whatsapp_inbound_messages.payload (the full Meta message, i.e. raw text like '850 groceries hdfc'), whatsapp_pending_turns.payload, agent_pending_intents.raw_input, exchange_rates and whatsapp_rule_state.
- **Failure scenario:** A stolen or idle session token of a household admin (not the owner) calls rpc('erase_household_data') directly. No code is asked for and the whole ledger is gone. Separately, a user who erases expecting 'every financial row' gone still has months of WhatsApp-logged message text with amounts and merchants in whatsapp_inbound_messages, plus amount-bearing notifications, linked to the household.
- **Recommendation:** Enforce recency on the server. Require `(auth.jwt()->>'aal') = 'aal2'`, or an `amr` entry of method 'otp' with a timestamp within 10 minutes, checked inside erase_household_data, request_account_deletion and the delete-account function. Restrict erase to the owner if that is the product intent. Extend the erase to notifications, whatsapp_inbound_messages (null out payload), whatsapp_pending_turns, whatsapp_rule_state, agent_*, exchange_rates and ai_feedback. Test: a session without a recent OTP receives 42501, and after an erase none of those tables has rows or payloads for the household.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): verifyAccountActionCode (auth.ts:281-284) is browser-only. The latest erase_household_data (v1030_net_worth_snapshots.sql:119-149) checks only caller_role in owner/admin, with no aal/amr/recency check, which contradicts Privacy.tsx:152. The delete list leaves out notifications, whatsapp_inbound_messages.payload, whatsapp_pending_turns and agent_* tables. A stolen session already grants broad access, so this is defen…

### DB-SEC-09 · MEDIUM · Tenant-consistency triggers (S3) skip asset_id, initiated_by, schedule and account links

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S3
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260909120000_audit_s3_tenant_consistency.sql:16-19`, `supabase/migrations/20260909120000_audit_s3_tenant_consistency.sql:24-64`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:59-61`, `supabase/migrations/20260602120000_money_map_phase1_accounts.sql:30,60`, `supabase/migrations/20260908120300_audit_f2_record_loan_payment.sql:31-32`, `supabase/migrations/20260612120100_v91_feedback_batch.sql:94,110`
- **Evidence:** assert_txn_tenant_consistency checks account_id, to_account_id, member_id and debt_id only. It does not check transactions.asset_id (added later by R4 with `on delete restrict`), initiated_by, or recurring_schedule_id. accounts.asset_id, accounts.debt_id, recurring_schedules.owner_member_id and the txn_template accountId/toAccountId/assetId keys have no tenant check at all. The S3 header says the functions run in 'the table owner's definer context', but none is SECURITY DEFINER. That fails closed and is safe, but the comment is wrong.
- **Failure scenario:** A member of household A inserts an investment {household_id: A, account_id: <A's bank>, asset_id: <B's mutual-fund asset>} (an id learnt as an ex-member or from a shared screen). Because of ON DELETE RESTRICT, household B can no longer be deleted (the cascade hits the restrict), B's erase_household_data fails on `delete from assets`, and delete-account for B's owner errors. B cannot see the blocking row because it lives in A.
- **Recommendation:** Extend assert_txn_tenant_consistency to asset_id, initiated_by and recurring_schedule_id. Add equivalent triggers on accounts (asset_id, debt_id) and recurring_schedules (owner_member_id plus the template ids). Correct the header comment. Test: inserting a transaction whose asset_id belongs to another household raises 23503.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): assert_txn_tenant_consistency (audit_s3:24-60) is the only definition and checks only account_id, to_account_id, member_id and debt_id. transactions.asset_id was added later with ON DELETE RESTRICT (r4:59-60) and nothing tenant-checks it, so a foreign asset id blocks B's erase and its household cascade. The attacker needs B's asset UUID. The header's 'definer context' claim is false: no function is SECURITY DEFINER,…
- **Also reported as:** DB-INT-10 (Tenant-consistency trigger predates asset_id: transactions can reference another househol…); INT-TXN-16 (Tenant-consistency trigger does not cover transactions.asset_id or recurring_schedule_id)

### DB-SEC-10 · MEDIUM · No database authorization test suite: RLS and RPC role/tenant matrix untested

- **Slice:** RLS, grants, SECURITY DEFINER, tenant isolation · **Category:** testing · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/tests/household_delete_activity_log_regression.sql:25-60`, `scripts/test-inventory-config.mjs:36`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/loanPaymentSql.test.ts`
- **Evidence:** supabase/tests contains one manual DO-block regression for household deletion. The only executable SQL test is the PGlite fixture for record_loan_payment (the 'sql-integration' group). Nothing exercises policies or SECURITY DEFINER functions per role (owner/admin/member/viewer/child/non-member/anon) or across households. That gap is why the critical replace_* and transfer_ownership defects above, and the cross-tenant ON CONFLICT write, ship despite the S2 and v10.22 hardening work.
- **Failure scenario:** The next migration redefines a function, or re-creates a view without security_invoker, or adds an UPDATE policy without WITH CHECK. CI is green and the hole ships, as it did for upsert_budget's guard, which exists only in production.
- **Recommendation:** Extend the existing PGlite harness, or add pgTAP, into a generated matrix. For every table: select/insert/update/delete as each role in an own and a foreign household. For every SECURITY DEFINER function: call as each role and as a non-member, asserting 42501 or success. Assert `has_function_privilege('anon', oid, 'EXECUTE')` is false for all prosecdef functions, and that every public table has RLS enabled. Run it in the db-migrations CI gate.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): supabase/tests holds only household_delete_activity_log_regression.sql. The only executable SQL test is the loanPaymentSql PGlite fixture (test-inventory-config.mjs 'Loan SQL command'). scripts/validate-new-migrations.mjs regex-checks some audit policy text statically, but nothing runs a per-role, cross-household RLS or SECURITY DEFINER matrix. The cited defects 'above' were not verified here.

## Integration

### INT-HH-03 · HIGH · Account deletion erases data, then fails on auth-user foreign keys and cannot be retried

- **Slice:** End-to-end: identity, households and membership · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:66-97`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:282`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:290`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:294`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:314`, `supabase/migrations/20260724130000_shared_splits.sql:88`, `supabase/migrations/20260724120000_fix_household_delete_activity_log_fk.sql:62-64`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:200-212`
- **Evidence:** Several columns reference auth.users with the default NO ACTION and are never altered: `transactions.created_by`, `activity_log.actor_id`, `invitations.invited_by`, `households.created_by` and `shared_split_shares.settled_user_id`. The function erases owned households, deletes the user's other memberships (L88-91), then deletes the profile (L93) and only then calls `admin.auth.admin.deleteUser` (L96). For non-owned households, the user's transactions and the activity_log rows written by `log_domain_activity` (`actor_id = auth.uid()` on every write) remain. Postgres therefore refuses to delete the auth user. A retry hits `profile_not_found` (L55) because the profile is already gone.
- **Failure scenario:** Rohan is a member of Priya's Family household and has logged 40 expenses there, which also wrote 40 activity_log rows with actor_id = Rohan. He chooses 'Delete immediately'. His own Personal household is erased, his memberships and profile are deleted, then deleteUser fails with a foreign-key violation and the UI shows 'Could not delete account'. Retrying returns 404 profile_not_found forever. His login still exists with no profile and no household, his name still sits on 40 transactions, and the erasure promise is broken.
- **Recommendation:** Move the whole purge into one SECURITY DEFINER SQL function that runs in a single transaction. It should erase owned households, delete memberships, null or anonymise created_by / actor_id / invited_by / settled_user_id / households.created_by, and leave the profile row to the auth cascade. Alternatively, migrate those FKs to `on delete set null`. Have the Edge Function call that RPC first and deleteUser last, and treat profile-missing plus auth-present as resumable. Add an integration test: a member with transactions in another household deletes their account, and both the auth row and the profile are gone.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): transactions.created_by, activity_log.actor_id, invitations.invited_by and households.created_by reference auth.users with NO ACTION (baseline:282,290,294,314), and shared_split_shares.settled_user_id does too (shared_splits:88). No later migration alters them. delete-account deletes the profile (L93) before deleteUser (L96), so a member with rows in another household fails at L96, and a retry hits profile_not_found…
- **Also reported as:** BE-FN-01 (Account deletion always fails for anyone who acted in a household they don't own, leaving…); DB-INT-06 (Account deletion half-completes: NO ACTION FKs to auth.users block deleteUser); DB-OPS-05 (delete-account fails at auth deleteUser on NO ACTION FKs after the profile is already gone); XC-PRV-06 (delete-account strands accounts via NO ACTION FKs and wipes co-members' data)

### INT-PAR-01 · HIGH · Server engine replaces the default FX rates with saved ones; the app merges them, so foreign rows convert at 1

- **Slice:** Server/browser logic parity and generated code · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:64`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:65`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:66`, `react/src/lib/dataAdapter.ts:346`, `react/src/lib/dataAdapter.ts:347`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:334`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:341`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:145`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:366`, `react/src/lib/money.ts:151`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/serverEngine.test.ts:64`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/serverEngine.test.ts:65`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:156`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:163`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:1003`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:8`
- **Evidence:** `shapesFromRows` has the comment "The store's rule: the household's saved rates when it has any, else the defaults" and does `const rates = Object.keys(saved).length ? saved : { ...DEFAULT_RATES }`, so saved rows REPLACE the defaults. That is not the store's rule. In cloud mode the store uses HybridAdapter (dataSlice.ts:145). Its `getRates` writes the cloud rows into the cache and returns `cache.getRates`, which is `{ ...DEFAULT_RATES, ...(await this.read('rates', ...)) }`, a MERGE. `convertViaUsdRates` then uses `rates[toCode] ?? 1`, so a missing rate silently becomes 1. The first household comes from `handle_new_user`, which inserts no exchange_rates rows. Editing one rate in Settings (`upsertRate(code, val)`) therefore leaves a one-row table. CON-UNIT-W4-001 pins the replace behaviour (`.toEqual({ USD: 1, INR: 84 })`) while calling it the app's rule. Every bundle entry point reads these rates: Pip on WhatsApp, `reconcileOnServer` (Reply UPDATE), `runwaySnapshot` and `notificationsOnServer`.
- **Failure scenario:** An INR household created by sign-up edits only EUR in Settings, so its saved rates are {EUR: 0.95}. It has a USD 120 expense on HDFC (opening ₹50,000). App: 120 × 83.20 = ₹9,984, so HDFC reads ₹40,016. Server: INR is missing, so the rate is 1 and the expense is ₹120; HDFC computes as ₹49,880. The user replies UPDATE with the statement figure ₹40,016. reconcileOnServer books delta = 40,016 − 49,880 = −₹9,864 into reconciliation_offset, and whatsapp_reconcile_account persists it. The app then shows HDFC at ₹30,152, and net worth is permanently understated by ₹9,864. The same households get understated foreign spend in Pip's WhatsApp answers and runway alerts. Pip also replies needs_rate for currencies the app converts fine.
- **Recommendation:** In serverEngine.shapesFromRows use `const rates = { ...DEFAULT_RATES, ...saved }`, mirroring LocalStorageAdapter.getRates. Better, export one `effectiveRates(saved)` from lib/ and call it from both the adapter and serverEngine. Fix CON-UNIT-W4-001 to expect the merge. Add a test where rows.rates = [{EUR, 0.95}] and the ledger has a USD row, and assert that contextFromRows(...).summary and reconcileOnServer(...) equal what the store path computes (HybridAdapter + buildSafeSummary). Rebuild the bundle.
