### INT-TXN-01 · HIGH · Editing a split from Transactions/Dashboard erases the split; deleting it there leaves the shared split live

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/pages/Transactions.tsx:572`, `react/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:307`, `react/src/pages/FormPages.tsx:57-61`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:379`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:388-428`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:483-493`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:201-235`, `react/src/lib/calculations.ts:26-30`
- **Evidence:** Both the Transactions list (`onEdit={openEditTxn}`) and the Dashboard send every row to `/transactions/:id/edit`, including split-backed rows. `TransactionEdit` never redirects a split to `/splits/:id/edit`. `persist()` builds a new `Transaction` from the form fields only: there is no `split`, `recurringScheduleId`, `debtId` or `initiatedBy`. The comment at line 379 says it outright: "a plain transaction never carries a `split`". `txnToRow` then writes a full row: `extras: { time, paymentMethod, excluded, linkedDebtId, linkedTxnId, split: t.split, emi_split }` replaces the jsonb wholesale, and it also sets `recurring_schedule_id: fkOrNull(undefined)` (null) and `debt_id: null`. Spend reads `t.split?.isSplit ? t.split.yourShare : t.amount`. `del()` on that page calls only `removeTransaction(initial.id)`. It never calls `deleteSharedSplitForTxn`, which SplitFormModal and Splits.tsx do call.
- **Failure scenario:** A ₹3,000 dinner is split 3 ways, so yourShare is ₹1,000. The user opens it from Transactions and fixes a typo in the note, then saves. extras.split is gone, so spend and the budget now count ₹3,000 instead of ₹1,000 (+₹2,000), on every device after sync. If the user instead deletes the row from that page, the transaction is soft-deleted but shared_splits/shares stay open. The other two participants keep seeing that they owe ₹1,000 each for an expense that no longer exists, and they can still settle it.
- **Recommendation:** Make openEditTxn and TransactionEdit redirect to formPath.splitEdit when `txn.split?.isSplit`. Make the edit save a patch: start from `{...initial}` and overlay only the form's fields (the same rule `upsertAccount` and `accountToRow` follow), and/or have `txnToRow` omit `extras.split`, `recurring_schedule_id` and `debt_id` when they are undefined rather than nulling them. Route every delete of a split-backed txn through the split delete path. Tests: a unit test that upsertTransaction with a form-shaped payload on a split row keeps `extras.split` and `effectiveAmount`; an e2e test for Transactions → edit split → delete that asserts the shared_split is gone.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): modalSlice.ts:65 sends every row to transactionEdit, and TransactionEdit (FormPages.tsx:57-61) never checks split. persist() (TransactionFormModal.tsx:388-428) builds the txn with no split, recurringScheduleId or debtId. dataSlice upsertTransaction does no merge, and txnToRow (supabaseAdapter.ts:225-232) replaces extras, so the split is erased. del() calls only removeTransaction. shared_splits.txn_id is ON DELETE SE…
- **Also reported as:** FE-UIC-01 (Editing a split from the Transactions list erases the split; full bill becomes your spend)

### INT-TXN-04 · HIGH · computeNextDueDate overflows month-end: schedules on the 29th–31st skip whole months

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:94-121`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:172-185`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:56-72`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:134-136`
- **Evidence:** Monthly advance does `next.setMonth(next.getMonth() + 1); if (dayOfMonth) next.setDate(dayOfMonth);`. Moving from Jan 31 to month+1 gives 'Feb 31', which JS normalises to Mar 3, and setDate(31) then lands on Mar 31. The server port does the same in UTC (`setUTCMonth` then `setUTCDate`) and is parity-pinned to the client, so WhatsApp approvals advance identically. `firstDueOnOrAfter` clamps to the last day correctly, but only on save. Every later advance goes through `computeNextDueDate`.
- **Failure scenario:** Rent of ₹30,000 on the 31st posts on Jan 31, Mar 31, May 31, Jul 31, Aug 31, Oct 31 and Dec 31. February, April, June, September and November are never posted or reminded, which is ₹150,000 of rent missing from a year's cash flow, budgets and Pip answers. A salary on the 30th skips February every year. A 29th skips February in non-leap years. In a negative-UTC-offset zone, local-time arithmetic adds a further one-day drift at DST when dayOfMonth is unset (the server port's header admits this).
- **Recommendation:** Compute the target (year, month+1) and then `min(dom, lastDayOfMonth)` in UTC, reusing the `firstDueOnOrAfter` maths. Change lib/recurring.ts and _shared/recurring.ts in the same PR and keep recurringPort.test.ts green. Tests: dom 29/30/31 across Feb and 30-day months; and `advanceSchedule` run 12 times yields 12 distinct months.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): lib/recurring.ts:109-111 calls setMonth(+1) and then setDate(dom). From Jan 31 that gives Feb 31, which becomes Mar 3 and then Mar 31, so February is skipped, and April goes the same way. _shared/recurring.ts:65-67 repeats this in UTC and advancedDueDate uses it. firstDueOnOrAfter's clamp applies only on save; every later advance goes through computeNextDueDate.
- **Also reported as:** FE-MNY-02 (computeNextDueDate skips months for days 29-31 and double-posts in negative-UTC zones); BE-WA-02 (Monthly schedules on day 29–31 skip months (server port mirrors client overflow bug)); DB-INT-07 (computeNextDueDate overflows month-end: bills on the 29th-31st skip months); INT-PAR-03 (Advancing a monthly schedule on day 29-31 skips short months while creating one clamps; p…); INT-TZC-01 (Recurring advance (computeNextDueDate) skips short months for day 29-31 and double-posts …); GATE-01 (Monthly schedules on day 29-31 skip whole months: advanceSchedule overflows setMonth)

### INT-TXN-05 · HIGH · 'Daily' schedules violate both DB CHECKs: never sync, post device-only money every day

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** TD-31 (related: the multi-leg recurring event is not atomic)
- **Where:** `react/src/pages/Recurring.tsx:262-267`, `supabase/migrations/20260607160000_v89_recurring_schedules.sql:14`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:107`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:137-151`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:153-162`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:112`
- **Evidence:** The form offers `{ key: 'daily', label: 'Daily' }`. `recurring_schedules.frequency` is `check (frequency in ('weekly','monthly','yearly','custom_day'))`, and no migration relaxes it. `transactions.recurring` is `check (recurring in ('','weekly','monthly','yearly'))`, also never relaxed. `generateTransaction` writes `recurring: schedule.frequency` ('daily'), and `occurrenceRow` does the same server-side. Both writes fail with 23514. The client classifies that as transient: 4 retries (~30 s) that block every later op for that household+entity, then dead-letter. The engine posts the occurrence and advances the schedule as two independent outbox ops.
- **Failure scenario:** The user creates 'Milk ₹60 daily'. The schedule insert is rejected, so it exists only on that device's cache. Every app load there, the engine posts a ₹60 expense that is also rejected. After a month that device shows ₹1,800 of spend that the partner's device, the server engine, Pip on WhatsApp and Net Worth snapshots never see, and the 'N changes failed to sync' banner keeps growing.
- **Recommendation:** Either add 'daily' to both CHECKs in a migration (schema ships first, per CLAUDE.md) or remove Daily from FREQ_TABS and the RecurrenceFreq type. Make the occurrence write and the schedule advance one server command (insert-if-absent + advance, like whatsapp_approve_recurring) so a rejected leg cannot consume the occurrence. Tests: an SQL test inserting a daily schedule and its occurrence; a unit test asserting `generateTransaction().recurring` is in the DB allow-list.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): FREQ_TABS offers 'daily' (Recurring.tsx:262-267). recurring_schedules.frequency CHECK (v89:14) and transactions.recurring CHECK (baseline:107) both exclude 'daily', and no migration relaxes either. generateTransaction writes recurring='daily', and txnToRow passes it through. classifyFlushError (sync/conflict.ts:16-25) retries up to MAX_RETRIES and then dead-letters, so both the schedule and its occurrences stay on t…
- **Also reported as:** DB-INT-08 ('daily' schedules are refused by the recurring_schedules CHECK and never sync); INT-CON-03 (Daily recurring schedules are rejected by the DB CHECK and silently never sync)

### INT-TXN-06 · HIGH · Recurring engine runs on stale cache and writes last-write-wins: reposts deleted bills, reverts edits

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** concurrency · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:206`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:272`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:121-133`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:27-84`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:103-168`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:1119-1124`
- **Evidence:** `init()` calls `refresh()`. With a cursor present, `HybridAdapter.list` returns the CACHED rows and pulls the cloud in the background, and `runRecurringEngine()` then runs on those stale `recurringSchedules`/`transactions`. Its writes carry no precondition: `adapter.upsert('transactions', …, txn)` and `adapter.upsert('recurring', …, advanced)`, which are INSERT … ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE. `upsertRecurring` builds `next` without `updated_at`, so `next.id && next.updated_at` is always false and every schedule save is last-write-wins (TD-03 never applied to recurring). `advanced` spreads the stale schedule (`active`, template, amount).
- **Failure scenario:** (a) Partner A pauses Netflix (₹649, due today). Partner B opens the app with last week's cache: B's engine posts ₹649 and writes the schedule back with active:true, un-pausing it in the cloud. (b) A's device auto-posted electricity at the template ₹2,000 and A corrected it to the real ₹2,340. B's stale pointer regenerates the same deterministic id, ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE puts the amount back to ₹2,000, and the ₹340 edit is silently lost. (c) A deleted the schedule. B still posts one more occurrence (a new live row).
- **Recommendation:** Do not materialise from cache. Gate the engine on a cloud-confirmed read of 'recurring' and 'transactions' (as positionIsCloudFresh does for snapshots), or move auto-posting to a server command/cron that locks the schedule (`FOR UPDATE`, like whatsapp_approve_recurring) and inserts the occurrence with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING. Thread `updated_at` through upsertRecurring (`...s` then override) so schedule writes are guarded. Test: a hybrid adapter test whose cache holds an active due schedule while the cloud has it paused/deleted asserts the engine writes nothing.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): init awaits refresh() and then runRecurringEngine. When a cursor exists, HybridAdapter.list returns the cached rows and pulls the delta in the background (hybridAdapter.ts:121-133). The engine's adapter.upsert calls for the transaction and the advanced schedule (recurringSlice.ts:153-162) pass no expectedUpdatedAt, so they take the ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE path (supabaseAdapter.ts:1118-1122). recurringToRow writes acti…
- **Also reported as:** FE-SYN-02 (Recurring engine on a stale cache overwrites edited occurrences and reverts paused or edi…)

### INT-TXN-07 · HIGH · loan_emi stored as a plain full-amount expense by recurring, WhatsApp log and WhatsApp correct

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit F2 (fixed for the form; still open for these writers)
- **Where:** `react/src/pages/Recurring.tsx:374`, `react/src/constants.ts:66`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:92-98`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:153-156`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:579-600`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:121`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:339-350`, `supabase/migrations/20260928120000_w3_whatsapp_capture_conversation.sql:147-148`
- **Evidence:** CLAUDE.md: "loan_emi is a SYSTEM_SPLIT". The Recurring form's `CategoryPicker type={type}` offers every EXPENSE category, including `loan_emi`, and has no loan picker, so templates have no `linkedDebtId`. The engine writes `adapter.upsert('transactions', …, txn)` directly, bypassing `upsertTransaction`, even though the dataSlice comment claims the safety net 'catches any other create path (recurring engine, imports)'. Approval goes through upsertTransaction, but its net requires `t.linkedDebtId`. On WhatsApp the parser maps `emi`/`loan`/`loan payment` to `loan_emi`, and `whatsapp_log_transaction` inserts it as an ordinary expense. `whatsapp_correct_last` will relabel any expense to `loan_emi` (`categoryFitsType('loan_emi','expense')` is true). Only whatsapp_approve_recurring refuses it.
- **Failure scenario:** A ₹25,000 EMI (₹18,000 interest + ₹7,000 principal) is logged as "emi 25000" on WhatsApp or by an auto-posting schedule. It is booked as ₹25,000 of spend, with no principal transfer and no debt re-amortisation. Monthly spend is overstated by ₹7,000 and the loan balance/liabilities are overstated by ₹7,000 more each month (₹84,000 after a year), across the Dashboard, Net Worth and Pip.
- **Recommendation:** Treat loan_emi as command-only everywhere. Exclude it from the Recurring CategoryPicker, or require a debt link and route through record_loan_payment with a deterministic operationId (e.g. the occurrence id) so double approval cannot double-post. Have the engine refuse it or call upsertTransaction. Make whatsapp_log_transaction and whatsapp_correct_last return a 'record_emi_in_app' status. Add a DB guard: category='loan_emi' requires `extras ? 'emi_split'` or `debt_id`. Tests: one CON test per writer asserting rejection.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): categoryOptions does not filter loan_emi (constants.ts:66), and the Recurring CategoryPicker has no debt link. The engine calls adapter.upsert directly, and the upsertTransaction safety net needs linkedDebtId. Parser line 121 maps emi to loan_emi. Latest whatsapp_log_transaction (r4:339-350) inserts it as a plain expense. whatsapp_correct_last (w3:131-155) and categoryFitsType both allow loan_emi.
- **Also reported as:** FE-MNY-03 (A recurring 'Loan / EMI payment' posts as a plain full-amount expense, bypassing the syst…); XC-ARC-02 (Auto-approved recurring EMI is posted as a plain full-amount expense, bypassing loan_emi …)

### INT-TZC-02 · HIGH · Base-currency change relabels account opening balances and offsets without converting them

- **Slice:** End-to-end: time zones, dates and currency · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit F4 (account-balance units are not explicit) — still open; R1 formalised the relabel instead of fixing it
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260910120000_r1_accounts_one_cash_household_currency.sql:179-218`, `react/src/lib/accountBalance.ts:52-69`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:532-539`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:650-704`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:416-447`, `react/src/lib/helpContent.ts:226`
- **Evidence:** The trigger households_currency_to_accounts runs `update public.accounts set currency = new.base_currency where household_id = new.id`, and its comment says 'Amounts are untouched ... computeAccountBalance treats opening balances and offsets as base-currency figures.' computeAccountBalance starts from `(account.openingBalance ?? 0) + (account.reconciliationOffset ?? 0)` with no conversion, while every transaction goes through effectiveAmount(t, baseCurrency, rates), which converts it. Settings changes the base currency on a single <Select onChange={e => updateProfile({ baseCurrency: e.target.value })}>, with no confirmation and no guard. updateProfile then writes households.base_currency and mirrors the relabel locally (dataSlice.ts:696-701). The R1 migration itself refused to relabel accounts holding money ('relabelling would change what the amount means'), but the trigger it installed does exactly that on every later change. The credit-card limit, opening balances and offsets all switch unit.
- **Failure scenario:** An INR household has Cash in Hand with an opening balance of ₹20,000 and HDFC with ₹1,50,000. Its INR transactions net −₹40,000. The user switches Base Currency to USD (rates: INR 83.2). Balance = 20,000 + 150,000 opening, now read as dollars, minus 40,000/83.2 = $480.77, giving $169,519 (about ₹1.41 crore) instead of $1,563. Net Worth, Spendable now, Ask Vyact liquidity and affordability all read it. If this is the first app open of a month, record_net_worth_snapshot permanently stores the inflated figure (first write wins). A reconcile done while in USD writes an offset in the wrong unit, which stays wrong after switching back.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the relabel trigger with a single SECURITY DEFINER RPC `change_base_currency(p_household, p_new, p_rate)`. It should either refuse (as R1 did) while any account has a non-zero opening_balance, reconciliation_offset or credit_limit, or convert those fields (plus budget allocations and the thresholds held in base units) at a stated rate in the same transaction, with a dated log entry. Add a confirmation step in Settings that states the conversion. Test: in cloud mode, compute net worth before and after an INR->USD switch; the results must agree within rounding at the stated rate.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): No later migration redefines the trigger at r1 migration:197-212, which relabels account currency and leaves amounts unchanged. computeAccountBalance (accountBalance.ts:52-69) adds opening balance and offset unconverted, while each transaction goes through effectiveAmount. Settings.tsx:535-536 changes the currency in one tap with no guard. The helpContent.ts:226 caveat does not stop the switch.
- **Also reported as:** FE-MNY-09 (Changing base currency relabels account amounts but converts transactions, so balances mi…); DB-INT-12 (Base-currency change relabels account balances but not transactions, so balances mix units)

### INT-TZC-03 · HIGH · Client 'today' and 'this month' are UTC, so entries, bills and month boundaries use the wrong local day

- **Slice:** End-to-end: time zones, dates and currency · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/format.ts:6`, `react/src/lib/format.ts:70`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:86`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:123-131`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:501-502`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:608`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:124-126`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:309-318`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:43-44`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:94`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:105`, `react/src/pages/Recurring.tsx:143`, `react/src/lib/reportRange.ts:45-62`, `react/src/lib/calculations.ts:205-208`, `react/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:68`, `react/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:115`, `react/src/pages/Budgets.tsx:87`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:427`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:738`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:753`, `react/src/pages/Splits.tsx:117`
- **Evidence:** `export const today = (): string => new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0];` and `nowMonthKey = () => today().slice(0, 7)` are UTC. There is no local-date helper anywhere in src (grep for getTimezoneOffset / timeZone finds only display formatting). The transaction form defaults `date: today()` and its 'Today' chip sets todayStr = today(). A comment there even pins yesterdayStr to 'today()'s UTC basis'. The recurring engine decides what is due with dueSchedules(now = today()), and approveRecurring refuses `occurrenceDate > today()`. Reports clamps every range `to` today(). The Dashboard's cash flow uses `mk = nowMonthKey()` (UTC), but the same page's budget card selects `b.periodMonth === now.getMonth() + 1` (LOCAL), as do budgetWindow and Budgets.tsx. Net-worth snapshots, debt payments, split 'paidOn' and new schedules' startDate all use the UTC day.
- **Failure scenario:** India (UTC+5:30), 00:45 on 1 Nov: tapping 'Today' stores 2026-10-31. A ₹1,200 dinner lands in October's cash flow, October's budget and the October month-close, and the Dashboard still calls October 'this month' until 05:30, while Budgets (local) already shows November. New York, 21:00 EDT on 31 Oct: 'Today' = 2026-11-01. The Dashboard cash-flow tile flips to an empty November while the budget card on the same screen still shows October. An auto-confirm rent due 1 Nov posts at 20:00 local on 31 Oct, and an approval bill can be approved a day early. A user in India at 02:00 who hand-picks the real local date (5 Oct) has that entry excluded from every Reports panel, because the range is clamped to 4 Oct.
- **Recommendation:** Introduce localToday() and localMonthKey() in format.ts built from local getters (or from a stored household zone), and use them wherever 'now' is meant. Keep UTC only for pure YYYY-MM-DD arithmetic. Then replace today()/nowMonthKey() and the inline `new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0]` sites listed. Tests: use vi.setSystemTime with process.env.TZ='Asia/Kolkata' at 2026-10-31T19:15Z and 'America/New_York' at 2026-11-01T01:00Z. Assert the form default, dueSchedules, Dashboard mk and budget month all agree on the local day.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): format.ts:6 builds today() from toISOString (UTC), and nowMonthKey derives from it. Meanwhile nowTime() (format.ts:7-10) is LOCAL, so in IST from 00:00 to 05:30 an entry gets yesterday's date with today's time. Dashboard.tsx:68 uses the UTC mk, but :115 picks the budget by local getMonth. dueSchedules/approveRecurring and resolveRange clamp to UTC today. No local-date helper exists in src.
- **Also reported as:** FE-MNY-05 ('Today' and 'this month' are UTC, so entries and month figures land on the wrong local da…); FE-UIC-03 (Default dates are UTC while times are local: entries land on the wrong day and month)

### INT-HH-07 · MEDIUM · Owners and admins can add any user to a household without consent and read their profile

- **Slice:** End-to-end: identity, households and membership · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit S2 ('whether user identity and household linkage can change' still open)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260908120100_audit_s2_membership_role_grants.sql:23-38`, `supabase/migrations/20260601120000_td06_td21_rls_perf_and_delta_sync_indexes.sql:103-112`, `supabase/migrations/20260908120000_audit_s1_whatsapp_identities.sql:26-27`, `supabase/migrations/20260614120000_whatsapp_connection_foundation.sql:15-17`, `supabase/migrations/20260724130000_shared_splits.sql:88`
- **Evidence:** The INSERT policy on memberships checks only the caller's role and the granted role, never `user_id`. An owner or admin can insert a row for any auth uid, skipping the invitation flow, and can UPDATE an existing member row's `user_id`. `profiles_read` grants SELECT on any profile that shares a household with the caller. The S1 migration left the legacy `profiles.phone_number / phone_verified_at / whatsapp_household_id` columns populated ('left in place (deprecated)'), so a profile row still exposes a verified phone number. Uids leak to other users, for example `shared_split_shares.settled_user_id` is visible to the split owner.
- **Failure scenario:** Eve shares a split with Dan. When Dan settles, Eve's client reads `settled_user_id` (Dan's uid). Eve inserts `{household_id: EveHH, user_id: Dan.uid, role: 'viewer', display_name: 'x'}` into memberships, then selects Dan's profile: display name, phone_number, deletion status. Dan now also sees Eve's household in his household list without ever having accepted anything.
- **Recommendation:** Restrict the INSERT policy so a client can only insert `user_id = auth.uid()` (or remove client INSERT entirely, since the accept RPC and the handle_new_household trigger run as definer). Add a WITH CHECK on UPDATE forbidding changes to user_id and household_id. Null out the deprecated profiles phone columns and drop them once the deploy window has passed. Test: an owner inserting a membership for another uid is rejected.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The INSERT WITH CHECK (S2 migration:24-27) and the UPDATE policy (30-38) never constrain user_id, so an owner can enrol any uid without consent. profiles_read (td06:103-112) then exposes the victim's profile row. The S1 migration freezes the legacy phone columns but leaves them populated. settled_user_id is readable by the split owner (shared_splits.sql:143-146). The member-limit trigger only caps the count.
- **Also reported as:** DB-SEC-07 (Owners/admins can enrol any user without consent, which exposes that user's profile (incl…)

### INT-HH-08 · MEDIUM · Household settings permissions disagree with RLS; partial writes and unhandled errors

- **Slice:** End-to-end: identity, households and membership · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/permissions.ts:31-37`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:763`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:780-809`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:527`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:536`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:597`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:309`, `react/src/pages/Debts.tsx:137`, `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:349`, `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:379-385`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:189-194`
- **Evidence:** `can('admin','edit_household_settings')` is true, but the only households UPDATE policy is `role_in(id) = 'owner'`. `updateProfile` writes `profiles` first and then `households`, and throws `WriteNotAppliedError` when 0 rows change. `language` is mapped to BOTH tables (L787 and L802). The Language, Base Currency and Payoff Strategy selects and the Debts payoff toggle are not role-gated and use `onChange={e => updateProfile(...)}` with no catch. The onboarding sync's `updateHousehold` for non-owners fails and is only console.warn'ed.
- **Failure scenario:** A household 'member' changes Language to Hindi. profiles.language is updated, the households update matches 0 rows, and WriteNotAppliedError surfaces as an unhandled promise rejection. The store never applies the new profile, so the UI shows the old language while the DB holds the new one. An Admin tries to rename the household or switch it to Family: the controls are enabled for them, but the write is refused. A member's onboarding progress never persists to households.onboarding, so a second device re-runs onboarding.
- **Recommendation:** Decide the policy: either allow owner and admin in a households UPDATE policy (with WITH CHECK, and keeping the type trigger), or remove 'edit_household_settings' from admin. Split user-level and household-level writes so `language` is not written to households by non-owners. Gate household-level controls with can(). Wrap the onChange calls in try/catch with a toast. Test: an admin rename succeeds or the button is disabled; a member language change succeeds.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Mismatch is real: admin has edit_household_settings (permissions.ts:35), but households UPDATE is owner-only (baseline:763). updateProfile writes language to both tables (supabaseAdapter.ts:787,802), and profileFromRows prefers hh.language, so a member's change reverts. But in cloud mode the write goes through the HybridAdapter outbox (hybridAdapter.ts:314-321) and ends in retries and a dead-letter, not the describe…

### INT-HH-09 · MEDIUM · Invite acceptance is not atomic or single-use; stale tokens and legacy RPCs linger

- **Slice:** End-to-end: identity, households and membership · **Category:** concurrency · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260601153000_fix_shareable_invite_accept_rpc.sql:19-54`, `supabase/migrations/20261007120000_household_types_v2.sql:52-73`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:412-459`, `react/src/pages/auth/AcceptInvite.tsx:21-41`, `react/src/lib/postAuth.ts:29-37`, `react/src/pages/auth/SignIn.tsx:11-14`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:112-118`, `react/src/lib/householdTypes.ts:46-49`
- **Evidence:** accept_invitation_link reads `invitations where token = ... and accepted_at is null` with no `FOR UPDATE`, checks for an existing membership, inserts, then sets accepted_at. memberships has no unique (household_id, user_id). The member-limit trigger counts rows without locking the household. An already-member opening the link marks it accepted, burning the link. `pending_invite_token` is written to sessionStorage and never removed, and both postAuthPath and SignIn redirect to it on every later sign-in in that tab. `listInvitations` does not filter expired rows, but `inviteBlockedReason` counts them as pending (the DB counts only `expires_at > now()`). The legacy accept_invitation(text) and accept_invitation(uuid) overloads remain executable by authenticated users; the uuid one compares a text column to uuid.
- **Failure scenario:** A Couple household (1 member) shares one link in a family chat. Two people tap it at the same moment. Both transactions see accepted_at null and count = 1 < 2, so both insert, giving a Couple household with 3 members and a token used twice. On a shared tablet, A opens an invite while signed out and leaves. B signs in in the same tab, is redirected to /invite/<token>, and auto-joins A's intended household.
- **Recommendation:** In accept_invitation_link, `select ... for update` the invitation and `perform 1 from households where id = inv.household_id for update` before counting. Use `update invitations set accepted_at = now() where id = inv.id and accepted_at is null` and check row_count. Add a unique index on memberships(household_id, user_id). Don't burn the token for an existing member. Clear `pending_invite_token` in AcceptInvite before the RPC. Filter expired invites in listInvitations. Drop the two legacy accept_invitation overloads. Add a concurrency test with two sessions accepting the same token.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): accept_invitation_link (20260601153000:19-54) has no FOR UPDATE and no single-use update guard. The limit trigger (household_types_v2:58) counts unlocked rows, so concurrent accepts can overfill a Couple household. pending_invite_token is set (AcceptInvite.tsx:27) and never removed (no removeItem anywhere), and postAuth.ts:33 and SignIn.tsx:12 redirect to it on later sign-ins. listInvitations (auth.ts:112-118) does …

### INT-HH-12 · MEDIUM · 'Child (own data only)' invite role can read the whole household's finances

- **Slice:** End-to-end: identity, households and membership · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:736`, `react/src/lib/permissions.ts:47-51`, `supabase/migrations/20260601120000_td06_td21_rls_perf_and_delta_sync_indexes.sql:48-49`, `supabase/migrations/20260601120000_td06_td21_rls_perf_and_delta_sync_indexes.sql:128-129`, `supabase/migrations/20260724120000_fix_household_delete_activity_log_fk.sql:62-64`
- **Evidence:** The invite form offers `<option value="child">Child (own data only)</option>`. Server-side, `transactions_read` (and the debts, assets, accounts and activity_log read policies) is `is_member(household_id)` for every role, and activity_log stores `to_jsonb(new)` full row images. No client code filters by role 'child' (no `role === 'child'` check anywhere in react/src). The 'own' restriction exists only for edit and delete in RLS.
- **Failure scenario:** Parents invite their 14-year-old as 'Child (own data only)' to log pocket-money spending. The teen sees every transaction, salary, debt, account balance and the activity feed's full row diffs, and can ask Pip about household net worth.
- **Recommendation:** Either relabel the role honestly ('Child: sees the household, edits only their own entries'), or implement read scoping: RLS `role_in(household_id) <> 'child' or created_by = auth.uid()` on transactions, and deny child on debts, assets, accounts and activity_log. Add an RLS test for a child reading another member's transaction.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): Households.tsx:736 offers 'Child (own data only)', but transactions_read is is_member(household_id) for every role (td06:48-49), and the activity_log, budgets and other read policies are the same. No restrictive or private policy exists in any migration, and no client code filters by role 'child'. permissions.ts:47-51 scopes only edit/delete. The label promises a read restriction the system does not implement.

### INT-PAR-02 · MEDIUM · Client computeNextDueDate uses local-time setters; wrong in DST and negative-offset zones, unlike the UTC server port

- **Slice:** Server/browser logic parity and generated code · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:94`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:101`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:104`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:108`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:112`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:113`, `react/src/lib/recurring.ts:172`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:17`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:20`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:56`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:66`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:99`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:158`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/recurringPort.test.ts:42`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/recurringPort.test.ts:53`, `react/vitest.config.ts:10`
- **Evidence:** The client parses a YYYY-MM-DD date as UTC midnight (`new Date(lastGenerated || startDate)`). It then steps with LOCAL setters (`next.setMonth(next.getMonth() + 1); if (dayOfMonth) next.setDate(dayOfMonth)`, and `next.getDay()` for weekly) and serialises with `toISOString()`. The server port does the same steps with `setUTCMonth/setUTCDate/getUTCDay`. Its header says local and UTC agree at +05:30 and admits "A household in a negative-offset zone would need this revisited". Elsewhere the client already concedes this: its v10.20.6 comment says "local-time setDate/setMonth on a UTC-parsed date drifts a day either side of the date line" (firstDueOnOrAfter is UTC), yet advanceSchedule still uses computeNextDueDate. The parity test REC-P-002 runs in the process TZ, and vitest.config pins none, so on CI (UTC) local equals UTC and it cannot fail. I reproduced the client and server logic in node: Europe/London, monthly dom 15 from 2026-03-15 → client 2026-04-14, server 2026-04-15. America/New_York → client 2026-04-16, server 2026-04-15. NY weekly Monday from 2026-09-07 → client 2026-09-15 (a Tuesday), server 2026-09-14.
- **Failure scenario:** A New York household's rent recurs on the 15th. After the first approval or auto-post (recurringSlice.ts:99/158 → advanceSchedule), nextDueDate becomes the 16th and stays there, and a Monday weekly bill moves to Tuesday for good. In London the spring-DST occurrence falls due on the 14th. The occurrence id `recurringInstanceId(id, date)` changes with the date, so a server approval and an app approval of the 'same' bill produce different rows. CLAUDE.md positions the app for "any household anywhere", which makes these households in scope.
- **Recommendation:** Make client computeNextDueDate pure UTC (setUTC*/getUTCDay), identical to _shared/recurring.ts, or have the server import the bundled client function so one copy exists. Run recurringPort.test.ts, recurring.test.ts and moneyModel suites under several zones: set process.env.TZ in a vitest setup file per project for UTC, Asia/Kolkata, Europe/London and America/New_York, and assert the London and NY cases above.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): react/src/lib/recurring.ts:94-113 parses the date as UTC but steps it with local setters (setMonth/setDate/getDay). _shared/recurring.ts:56-66 uses setUTC*. I reproduced it in node: London gives monthly 15th → 2026-04-14; New York gives 04-16 and weekly Monday → Tuesday 09-15. It runs on every approval and auto-post through advanceSchedule (recurringSlice.ts:99,158). vitest.config.ts pins no TZ, and the port's own h…

### INT-PAR-04 · MEDIUM · Three clocks: UTC today(), host-local getters (UTC on Deno) and IST localDay produce different days

- **Slice:** Server/browser logic parity and generated code · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/format.ts:6`, `react/src/lib/format.ts:70`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:103`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:104`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:147`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:151`, `react/src/lib/askVyactBackend.ts:138`, `react/src/lib/askVyactBackend.ts:149`, `react/src/lib/askVyactBackend.ts:375`, `react/src/lib/askVyactBackend.ts:383`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:206`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:253`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:322`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:666`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:222`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-dispatch/index.ts:62`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:71`, `react/src/components/transactions/TransactionFormModal.tsx:123`
- **Evidence:** The ledger convention is a UTC day: `today = () => new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0]` and `nowMonthKey = today().slice(0, 7)`, which the form relies on ("Must share today()'s UTC basis"). The bundled engine also reads the HOST's local clock: resolvePeriod (`monthKeyOf(now.getFullYear(), now.getMonth())`), resolveDayWindow ('today'/'yesterday'/'this week' from local getters), samePointLastMonth, the week-ahead bill (`setHours(0,0,0,0)`) and runwaySnapshot (`now.getMonth() - 1`). No `now` or zone is injected (answerOnServer passes only Date.now() as a phrasing seed). In an IST browser those helpers use IST; on Deno they use UTC. The WhatsApp webhook resolves stated dates against an IST localDay. For an undated entry it omits p_date, so the RPC uses `coalesce(p_date, current_date)` (Postgres UTC). The dispatcher computes `today` in IST (`localDay(now, 330)`) for digests, bills and budget periods.
- **Failure scenario:** At 01:00 IST on 1 Oct (19:30 UTC on 30 Sep):
(a) In the app, Pip's default period (`currentPeriod()`, UTC) is September while 'this month' through resolvePeriod is October. The browser disagrees with itself, and WhatsApp Pip (all UTC) says September.
(b) WhatsApp 'how much did I spend yesterday' resolves to 29 Sep on the server and 30 Sep in the browser, a different total under the same label.
(c) '450 lunch' sent at 00:30 IST is dated 30 Sep (UTC). The 20:30 IST digest for 1 Oct queries date = 1 Oct, and the 30 Sep digest went out the evening before, so the entry is never in any digest. At month end it lands in the previous month's month_close_summary.
- **Recommendation:** Give the engine an explicit clock and zone: contextFromRows(rows, { now, tz }) and notificationsOnServer(rows, keys, hid, { now, tz }), with a single `localToday(tz)` / `localMonthKey(tz)` in format.ts replacing both today() and the local getters. Store the household's zone (default Asia/Kolkata) and pass it from the webhook, dispatch and web-push. Have the webhook always send `p_date = localDay(message)`. Add tests that run the engine with now = 2026-09-30T19:30Z under TZ=UTC and TZ=Asia/Kolkata and assert the same period and day windows.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): format.ts:6 today() is UTC. askVyactParser.ts:103-151 resolvePeriod/resolveDayWindow and askVyactBackend.ts:149/375 use host-local getters. The webhook (index.ts:322) uses IST localDay but omits p_date when no date is stated (:666), and the RPC uses coalesce(p_date,current_date) in UTC (r4 migration:222). whatsapp-dispatch:62 computes the digest 'today' in IST. All of it holds in the 00:00-05:30 IST window.

### INT-PAR-05 · MEDIUM · stale_balance push rule flags never-stamped accounts as stale and ignores last_reconciled_at

- **Slice:** Server/browser logic parity and generated code · **Category:** ux · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:273`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:275`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:277`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:279`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:280`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:35`, `react/src/lib/accountsView.ts:137`, `react/src/lib/accountsView.ts:155`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:97`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:119`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-dispatch/index.ts:201`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:210`, `supabase/migrations/20260910120000_r1_accounts_one_cash_household_currency.sql:17`, `supabase/migrations/20260606120000_v8_onboarding_state.sql:65`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:245`, `supabase/functions/web-push/index.ts:139`
