So a fresh Cash in Hand or bank account counts as stale on day one. The rule never reads lastReconciledAt. The other definitions disagree with it. The Accounts screen (accountsView.ts:155) uses `lastReconciledAt ?? last log ?? createdAt`. Reply UPDATE (serverEngine.balancesToCheck) uses bank/credit_card/cash with `lastReconciledAt ?? createdAt`. The WhatsApp nudge uses bank/credit_card/cash with `last_reconciled_at ?? created_at`. stale_balance is P1, and the web-push cron raises it through notificationsOnServer every 15 minutes (weekly dedupe key).
- **Failure scenario:** A household signs up, skips onboarding, and adds HDFC today. Within 15 minutes the web-push dispatcher raises and pushes a P1 lock-screen notification: '2 balances may be out of date — Last updated over 30 days ago' (Cash in Hand + HDFC). Both claims are untrue, and it repeats every week until each account is reconciled in the app. The same household is told 'two' on push and 'one' (or a different set, since a card counts there and not in push) by WhatsApp's balance_stale_nudge and Reply UPDATE.
- **Recommendation:** Write one `staleAccounts(accounts, nowMs)` in lib/accountsView.ts: spendable kinds bank/credit_card/cash, `lastReconciledAt ?? lastLog ?? createdAt`, more than STALE_AFTER_DAYS. Use it in staleBalanceNotifs, serverEngine.balancesToCheck and (via the bundle) the dispatch staleBalanceNudge. Add a test that a just-created account is not stale in any of the three, and that the three return the same count for one fixture.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): notifications.ts:273-280 counts bank/cash/investment accounts and treats confirmedAt||lastLog||estimatedAt, or else 0, as last touched. It ignores lastReconciledAt and createdAt. confirmed_at has no DB default (v8 migration:65), and only reconcile/onboarding set it. So new accounts are stale on day one, pushed as P1 via notificationsOnServer (serverEngine.ts:245) and in the bell (notifySlice.ts:77). This disagrees w…

### INT-PAR-06 · MEDIUM · WhatsApp currency detection diverges from the app: 'dollars', 'euros', 'quid' are logged in base currency

- **Slice:** Server/browser logic parity and generated code · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:84`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:85`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:242`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:246`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:247`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:249`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:328`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:349`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:350`, `react/src/lib/askVyactParser.ts:374`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/whatsappParser.test.ts:150`
- **Evidence:** parseAmount strips `dollars?|bucks?|quid|rupees?|usd|gbp|eur` from the text before reading the number. detectCurrency only recognises ₹/inr/rs/rupee, €/eur, £/gbp, $/usd, then takes the FIRST 3-letter word and checks it against a list, else returns base. The app's statedCurrency (askVyactParser.ts:349-382) also maps `dollars?|bucks?` → USD, `euros?` → EUR, `pounds?|quid` → GBP, `dirhams?` → AED and `yen` → JPY, and treats `$` as the base currency when the base is itself a $ currency. The only WhatsApp currency test (whatsappParser.test.ts:150-155) covers symbols, so the two copies drift unseen. The 3-letter fallback also misses a code that is not the first 3-letter word ('450 tea aud' → 'tea', not AUD).
- **Failure scenario:** An INR household sends '20 dollars netflix' on WhatsApp. The amount is read as 20 (the word 'dollars' was stripped), detectCurrency finds no symbol or code, and the row is written as ₹20 instead of $20 (₹1,664 at the default rate). The month's entertainment spend is understated by about ₹1,644. Pip in the app, given the same text, captures USD 20. '50 euros dinner' and '10 quid taxi' fail the same way.
- **Recommendation:** Use statedCurrency's table in the WhatsApp parser: move it to a small dependency-free module both trees can import (as was done for whatsapp-parser), or copy it verbatim. Add a parity test over ~15 phrasings ('$20', '20 dollars', '20 bucks', '50 euros', '10 quid', '100 aed', '450 tea aud', 'rs 500') asserting the WhatsApp parser and statedCurrency return the same code for base INR and base USD.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp-parser.ts:85 strips 'dollars/bucks/quid' before reading the amount. detectCurrency (:242-249) only knows symbols plus inr/rs/rupee/eur/gbp/usd, and its 3-letter fallback takes the first word only. So '20 dollars netflix' gets p_currency = base (webhook:654). askVyactParser WORD_CURRENCY (:360-370) maps those words. The blast radius is limited because the confirmation reply shows the currency it logged.

### INT-PAR-08 · MEDIUM · Deployed engine bundle can drift from the app: Edge deploy is neither gated on CI nor checks the bundle

- **Slice:** Server/browser logic parity and generated code · **Category:** ops-release · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** TD-33
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:6`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:21`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:58`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:117`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:119`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:138`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:151`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:155`, `scripts/automation-run.mjs:85`, `scripts/automation-run.mjs:87`, `scripts/build-agent-engine.mjs:63`, `TECH_DEBT.md:1091`
- **Evidence:** The staleness gate `node scripts/build-agent-engine.mjs --check` runs only inside CI's automation job (automation-run.mjs:87). deploy.yml triggers independently on every push to main (`on: push: branches: [main]`). deploy-edge-functions `needs: db-migrations` only, and it neither runs --check nor waits for CI. CLAUDE.md notes there is no branch protection, so a red merge is not blocked. The app is published by Vercel in parallel (deploy.yml:6-12, 58-63). TD-33's recorded fix ("The consumer job now needs [db-migrations, deploy-edge-functions]") no longer exists, because the consumer job was removed. If db-migrations fails, the Edge deploy is skipped while Vercel ships the new app. Functions are deployed one at a time (webhook, dispatch, web-push), so a mid-step failure leaves functions on different bundle versions. The bundle header lists modules but carries no commit or version, so nothing can tell which app version WhatsApp is running.
- **Failure scenario:** A PR changes a money rule in aiSummary.ts and forgets to rebuild the bundle. CI fails, but it is merged anyway (nothing prevents it), and deploy.yml publishes the stale bundle. Alternatively a migration fails on deploy. Either way the app answers with the new rule and WhatsApp Pip, runway alerts and push notifications keep the old one indefinitely, with no alert because nothing compares versions.
- **Recommendation:** In deploy-edge-functions, add `npm ci --prefix react && node scripts/build-agent-engine.mjs --check` before any `functions deploy`, or trigger deploy via `workflow_run` on a successful CI. Stamp the git SHA (and a hash of the module list) into the bundle header and expose it in a cheap GET on web-push or whatsapp-dispatch. Add a post-deploy check that compares it with the commit Vercel built. Update TD-33's status, since its resolution no longer holds.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): deploy.yml:21-23 triggers on push to main. deploy-edge-functions (:117-159) needs only db-migrations and never runs build-agent-engine --check, which runs only in CI automation (automation-run.mjs:87-88). There is no branch protection. Vercel ships the app in parallel (:58-63). Functions deploy one by one, and the bundle header carries no SHA. TD-33's 'consumer needs edge' fix no longer exists.

### INT-TXN-08 · MEDIUM · Rejected writes are retried as transient, then dead-lettered while the optimistic row stays forever

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** TD-10 (retry cap resolved; terminal-error handling and rollback still open)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/sync/conflict.ts:16-25`, `react/src/lib/sync/outbox.ts:217-237`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:244-264`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:466-490`, `react/src/components/layout/SyncConflictBanner.tsx:54-71`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:204-229`
- **Evidence:** `classifyFlushError` separates only `ConcurrencyConflictError`. Every other error, including permanent Postgres rejections (23514 CHECK, 23503 FK/tenant trigger, 42501 RLS, 22P02, PGRST204, `WriteNotAppliedError`), is retried with backoff. `claimDue` adds the resource to `blocked` before checking `nextRetryAt`, so a poison op stalls every later op for that household+entity for about 30 s, each time. After MAX_RETRIES the op is moved to `sync_failed`, but the optimistic cache row written at `this.cache.upsert(...)` (or removed at `cache.remove`) is never rolled back. 'Dismiss' only calls `clearFailed()`. The delta sync can never correct it, because the server has no row to tombstone.
- **Failure scenario:** A write the server will never accept (the daily schedule above, a CHECK-violating legacy investment, a child editing or deleting another member's row) leaves this device showing a ₹X transaction, or hiding one, that no other device agrees with. After 'Dismiss' the divergence is permanent until a cache-epoch purge, and the device's balances and Net Worth snapshot differ from the cloud.
- **Recommendation:** Classify SQLSTATE classes 22/23/42 and PGRST2xx as terminal on the first failure. On a terminal failure or Dismiss, revert the optimistic leg: remove an inserted row, refetch the server version for an update, re-add for a remove. Only block the entity queue on ops for the same entity id, not the whole table. Tests: a flush with a mocked 23514 dead-letters after one attempt and the cache no longer holds the row; a later op for another id is not delayed.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Holds: classifyFlushError (sync/conflict.ts:16-25) only treats ConcurrencyConflictError as terminal, so a permanent rejection is retried up to MAX_RETRIES=5 (about 62s of backoff). claimDue (outbox.ts:119-123) blocks the whole entity queue meanwhile. Dead-lettering never rolls back the cache, and Dismiss only clears counters even though the copy says 'discard'. Overstated: the divergence is not permanent. manualRefr…

### INT-TXN-10 · MEDIUM · Delta sync still not guaranteed current: pulls overwrite pending local writes; commit-order cursor gap

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit F8 (pagination and composite cursor fixed; 'overlay pending local writes' still open; commit-order gap new)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:144-158`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:204-229`, `react/src/lib/hybridAdapter.ts:449-455`, `react/src/lib/supabaseAdapter.ts:1043-1067`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:358-360`, `supabase/migrations/00000000000001_production_state_baseline.sql:613`, `react/src/components/layout/SyncConflictBanner.tsx:58-61`
- **Evidence:** `applyCloudDelta` upserts every server row into the cache with no check for pending outbox ops on that id. `applyCloudList` does `cache.replaceAll(entity, hid, fresh)`, which drops unflushed local inserts. That path runs on cold start and after `manualRefresh`/`forceFullResync`, and the conflict banner's 'Refresh & re-apply' calls it first. The F7 fold is skipped whenever any other op for the entity is pending (`hasPendingEntity` is entity-wide, not per row). The cursor is `updated_at`, set by `set_updated_at()` to `now()`, which is the transaction START time. A row from a longer transaction (an RPC such as whatsapp_*, record_loan_payment, move_account) can commit after another device has already advanced its cursor past that timestamp.
- **Failure scenario:** The user edits three transactions offline, then reconnects. The visibility refresh's delta lands before the flush, the old server values overwrite the cache, and the screen shows the pre-edit amounts until a later refresh. Separately: a WhatsApp RPC starts at 10:00:00.100 and commits at .180, an app write commits at .150, and device B's delta at .160 sets its cursor to .150. The WhatsApp row (updated_at .100) is never pulled by B until a manual full resync.
- **Recommendation:** Overlay pending ops when applying cloud reads: skip or re-apply rows that have queued writes, and keep unflushed inserts on replaceAll. Re-read the delta with a safety overlap (cursor minus ~60 s; the cache upsert is idempotent), or use a commit-ordered sequence. Tests: a delta applied while an op for the same id is pending keeps the local value; an overlap read picks up a back-dated updated_at.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Mostly refuted: listSince (supabaseAdapter.ts:1043-1067) returns only rows changed after the cursor, so a delta cannot revert untouched rows the user edited offline. The real overwrite is in applyCloudList replaceAll during manualRefresh, and it is transient. The commit-order gap is real (set_updated_at uses now() = transaction start, baseline:358-360). It is rare, can hide a row on one device until a manual full re…

### INT-TXN-11 · MEDIUM · Split sharing is a separate online write that races the queued transaction and is never retried

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit 8.5 (still open)
- **Where:** `react/src/components/splits/SplitFormModal.tsx:316-335`, `react/src/lib/sharedSplits.ts:83-123`, `supabase/migrations/20260724130000_shared_splits.sql:62`, `supabase/migrations/20260909120000_audit_s3_tenant_consistency.sql:100-108`
- **Evidence:** `await upsertTransaction(txn)` only writes the cache and enqueues; the flush is fire-and-forget. `createSharedSplit` then inserts `shared_splits` with `txn_id` directly against the cloud. `txn_id uuid references transactions(id)`, and the split tenant trigger requires `exists (select 1 from transactions where id = new.txn_id …)`. If the transaction op has not landed (offline, a backed-off head op blocking the queue, or another tab holding the flush lock), the insert fails with 23503. The catch only toasts 'Split saved, but sharing failed', and nothing re-attempts it. Parent and shares are also two inserts, not one transaction.
- **Failure scenario:** The user records a ₹4,500 trip split with two friends while the transactions queue is backing off a failed op. The split row is rejected, the friends are never notified and never see they owe ₹1,500 each, and the owner's Splits page shows participants with no cloud share to settle.
- **Recommendation:** Create transaction + shared split + shares in one RPC (the 2026-09 audit 8.5 recommendation), or enqueue the share creation behind the transaction op in the outbox with retry. Test: a store test that creates a split while the transaction op is still pending ends with exactly one shared_split after the flush.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): SplitFormModal.tsx:316-335: `await upsertTransaction` only reaches adapter.upsert. That writes the cache and enqueues, with `void this.flushQueue()` (hybridAdapter.ts:244-256). createSharedSplit (sharedSplits.ts:83-123) then inserts directly. trg_split_tenant_consistency requires the transaction row to exist (20260909120000:100-108). The catch only toasts, and there is no retry or outbox entry. The parent and share …

### INT-TXN-12 · MEDIUM · WhatsApp correction checks category fit against the wrong message; alias by kind picks arbitrary account

- **Slice:** End-to-end: the life of a transaction · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:845-856`, `supabase/migrations/20260928120000_w3_whatsapp_capture_conversation.sql:130-155`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:264-272`
- **Evidence:** `correctLast` reads the latest inbound message of any kind (`.neq('wa_message_id', message.id).order('processed_at', desc).limit(1)`) and skips the check when it has no `parsed.type` (`if (type && !categoryFitsType(...))`). `whatsapp_correct_last` acts on a different row, the one `whatsapp_last_logged` picks, and checks only `v_type not in ('expense','income')` before `set category = p_category`. Separately, `whatsapp_log_transaction` resolves an alias with `lower(name) = lower(p_account_alias) or lower(kind) = lower(p_account_alias) limit 1` and no ORDER BY.
- **Failure scenario:** The user sends "salary 50000" (income), then "how much did I spend?", then "no, that was groceries". The middle message has no parsed type, so the check is skipped and the ₹50,000 income is stored under the expense category 'groceries', breaking type-scoped categories in income reports and Pip. "paid 2000 from bank" in a household with two bank accounts debits whichever row Postgres returns first, so per-account balances are wrong.
- **Recommendation:** Have whatsapp_correct_last validate the category against the locked row's type server-side (an allow-list per type, as in _shared/agent/types.ts), and drop the client-side guess. For aliases, refuse with 'ambiguous_account' when more than one account matches by kind (or prefer the default account), and add ORDER BY. Tests: an RPC test with an income row and an expense category returns 'wrong_type'; an alias test with two bank accounts.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:850-856 reads the latest inbound message of any kind and skips the type check when parsed.type is absent (for example a query). whatsapp_correct_last (w3:130-155) checks only expense/income on the row whatsapp_last_logged picked, and the DB has no per-type category check. The final whatsapp_log_transaction (r4:264-272) matches on lower(kind) with LIMIT 1 and no ORDER BY. The parser emits 'b…

### INT-TZC-04 · MEDIUM · WhatsApp: untagged entries use the database's UTC current_date while 'yesterday' uses IST; dispatch hard-codes IST

- **Slice:** End-to-end: time zones, dates and currency · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:320-327`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:604`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:651-667`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:201-214`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:222-225`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-dispatch/index.ts:61-62`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:70-73`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:275-293`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:541-547`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-dispatch/index.ts:232-256`
- **Evidence:** The webhook computes the sender's day as IST (`localDay`: Meta timestamp + VYACT_TZ_OFFSET_MINUTES default 330) and passes it to the parser, so 'yesterday' resolves to IST-today − 1. But for a message with no stated date it sends `...(tx.date ? { p_date: tx.date } : {})`, and the latest whatsapp_log_transaction does `v_date := coalesce(p_date, current_date)`, where current_date is the database's UTC date. The scheduler takes `today = localDay(now, 330)` for EVERY household. billsFor / billReminders / overdueBillReminders have no INR or zone gate, and the bills cron runs at '30 3 * * *' UTC (09:00 IST). The digest matches `r.date === input.today` using the IST day.
- **Failure scenario:** 01:00 IST on 1 Nov: '450 groceries' is stored as 2026-10-31 (UTC). 'yesterday 450 groceries' sent at the same moment is also 2026-10-31, so 'today' and 'yesterday' collapse. The entry is in October's month-close (sent 09:10 IST 1 Nov) and October's budget alert, and it never appears in any evening digest (31 Oct's went at 20:30, and 1 Nov's matches date = 11-01). For a New York USD household, the bills job fires at 23:30 EDT the evening before, with a 'due today' reminder carrying the IST date, and 'paid Rent' then approves with p_today = the IST date, a day before the user's due day.
- **Recommendation:** Always send p_date = localDay(message) from the webhook, or add p_today to the RPC and clamp against it rather than current_date. Add households.timezone (default 'Asia/Kolkata', settable from the client's Intl zone) and use it in localDay, the dispatcher and the cron fan-out, instead of the global VYACT_TZ_OFFSET_MINUTES. Tests: a webhook handler test at Meta timestamp 2026-10-31T19:30Z asserting p_date = 2026-11-01, and a dispatch-rule test for a non-IST household.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:322-327 resolves 'yesterday' from IST localDay, but :666 omits p_date for undated messages; latest RPC (r4_investments_to_assets.sql:222) uses UTC current_date. No later migration redefines it and no DB timezone is set. Dispatch index.ts:62 applies a global 330 offset to every household. billsFor/billReminders have no zone or INR gate; the bills cron is '30 3 * * *' (w2b:188).
- **Also reported as:** BE-WA-05 (WhatsApp entry dates use UTC/IST server clocks, not the sender's local day)

### INT-TZC-05 · MEDIUM · Due-day P1 pushes raised at 05:30 IST land in the default quiet hours and are then marked sent

- **Slice:** End-to-end: time zones, dates and currency · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** TD-22 (resolved v10.48.0) — this is a new defect in its implementation
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:77-84`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:126-131`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:234-246`, `supabase/functions/_shared/web-push-rules.ts:55-67`, `supabase/functions/_shared/web-push-rules.ts:84`, `supabase/functions/web-push/index.ts:150-170`, `react/src/lib/webPush.ts:99`
- **Evidence:** The server raises the app's notifications via the bundled generators. recurringDueNotifs uses `const now = today()`, the UTC date in Deno, so a due-day P1 is first raised on the first 15-minute run after 00:00 UTC, which is 05:30 IST. DEFAULT_PREFS sets quietStart '22:00' and quietEnd '07:00', and the device's prefs carry these defaults (webPush.ts:99). inQuietHours evaluates them in the device zone (Asia/Kolkata), so skipReason returns 'quiet_hours'. Then dispatch step 3 runs `update({ pushed_at: now })` for EVERY pending id, 'pushed or deliberately not'. Pending rows are selected with `.is('pushed_at', null)`, so the notification is never reconsidered after 07:00.
- **Failure scenario:** An Indian user with default settings has an approval bill (rent ₹25,000) due 5 Oct. At 05:30 IST the server raises recurring_due_confirm. The device is inside 22:00–07:00, so the push is skipped and stamped as pushed. At 07:00 nothing is sent, and the lock-screen 'bill due' push, the main purpose of TD-22, never arrives for the primary market. The same applies to any P1 (budget threshold, debt due) raised overnight.
- **Recommendation:** Do not stamp pushed_at for rows skipped only for quiet_hours. Either leave them pending (they are still within the 24 h PUSH_WINDOW) or record per-device deferral in push_deliveries and resend after quietEnd. Separately, raise date-driven notifications in the household's zone (see the UTC 'today' finding). Test: a web-push dispatch test where a P1 created at 00:05Z is skipped at 00:15Z for an Asia/Kolkata device and IS pushed on the 01:30Z run (07:00 IST).
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): format.ts:6 today() is the UTC ISO date. recurringDueNotifs (notifications.ts:127) raises a due-day P1 at 00:00Z, which is 05:30 IST. web-push-rules.ts:64-66 skips it as quiet_hours against the 22:00-07:00 defaults that webPush.ts:99 sends. web-push/index.ts:187-188 then stamps pushed_at on every pending id, and :154 selects only pushed_at IS NULL, so the notification is never retried after 07:00.
- **Also reported as:** BE-FN-08 (Bill-due and debt-due pushes for India are raised at 05:30 IST, inside default quiet hour…)

### INT-TZC-07 · MEDIUM · FX uses a hard-coded, undated 2024 rate table and revalues historical foreign rows at today's rate

- **Slice:** End-to-end: time zones, dates and currency · **Category:** money-correctness · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit F4 ('Decide whether historical reporting uses transaction-date FX or current valuation FX'; 'Reject unknown currency/rate data') — still open; the ad hoc FX math in Transactions/aiSummary is fixed
- **Where:** `react/src/constants.ts:198-201`, `react/src/lib/dataAdapter.ts:346-347`, `react/src/lib/serverEngine.ts:64-66`, `react/src/lib/money.ts:136-152`, `react/src/lib/calculations.ts:25-31`, `react/src/lib/accountBalance.ts:52-69`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:242-250`, `supabase/migrations/20260911120000_r4_investments_to_assets.sql:340-347`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:468-473`, `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:574-578`
- **Evidence:** The only rate source is DEFAULT_RATES (`INR:83.20, JPY:149.00 ...`), a static constant with no as-of date, or a user-typed exchange_rates row. Households created by the handle_new_user trigger get no rate rows (no insert in migrations; only createHousehold seeds them). The client merges `{ ...DEFAULT_RATES, ...saved }`, but the server engine uses `Object.keys(saved).length ? saved : { ...DEFAULT_RATES }`, so a household that saved one rate loses the defaults on the server. convertViaUsdRates uses `rates[fromCode] ?? 1`. Every balance and report converts each stored foreign row at the CURRENT table (effectiveDinero), so changing a rate moves account balances and closed months with no transaction. Foreign rows still enter: the WhatsApp parser maps '$', 'usd', '€' and others to that currency, and the RPC stores p_currency against an account in the household currency.
- **Failure scenario:** An INR household sends WhatsApp '$20 lunch'. A USD 20 row debits Cash, valued at 20×83.2 = ₹1,664, while the card actually charged ₹1,760. The user reconciles, so the offset is −₹96. Later they update INR to 88 in Settings: the same row now debits ₹1,760, Cash is off by ₹96 in the opposite direction, and last month's Reports totals change. Server/client drift: a household whose only saved rate is INR answers on WhatsApp with EUR rows converted at 1.0, while the app uses 0.92.
- **Recommendation:** Persist the base-currency value on every foreign row at entry (`fx_rate`, `amount_base`). Fold balances and reports from it, and use the live table only for valuing holdings. Give exchange_rates an as_of the UI shows, with a staleness warning. Use one merge rule shared by client and server (export a single ratesFor() used by both dataAdapter and serverEngine). Test: a rate change must not change computeAccountBalance for an account with a foreign-currency row.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): DEFAULT_RATES (constants.ts:198) is static. The client merges defaults (dataAdapter.ts:347, via hybridAdapter.ts:341), while serverEngine.ts:65 uses only saved rates when any exist, so EUR falls to `?? 1` (money.ts:141). No migration seeds exchange_rates; only createHousehold does (dataSlice.ts:470). effectiveDinero (calculations.ts:28) revalues every row at the current rate. The WhatsApp parser (:242-250) stores US…

## Platform & Ops

### OPS-01 · HIGH · Production release is ungated: Vercel ships the app regardless of CI, migrations or Edge deploy

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** ops-release · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit 10.1 (still open); TD-33 (marked resolved, regressed when the consumer job was removed)
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:21-24`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:58-63`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:117-119`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:17-19`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:116-120`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:159-161`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:48-51`, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:18-23`, `TECH_DEBT.md:1091-1101`
- **Evidence:** deploy.yml runs on `push: branches: [main]` with no `needs` on any CI job. Its own comment (58-63) says the app is published by Vercel 'on the same push, in parallel, whatever happens here' and 'stays live if the migration fails'. In ci.yml the E2E Lane A and Lane B jobs are `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'`, so a push to main reruns only the cheap gates. That run also uses `cancel-in-progress: true` keyed on `github.ref`, so the next merge cancels it. ENVIRONMENTS.md:22 says 'Nothing makes the app wait for its migration.' Schema-first and function-first ordering are a human convention: no check rejects a PR that touches both `supabase/migrations` or `supabase/functions` and `react/src`. TD-33 is marked resolved because 'The consumer job now needs: [db-migrations, deploy-edge-functions]' (TECH_DEBT.md:1100), but that job was deleted on 1 Oct 2026 (deploy.yml:9-12). The race TD-33 fixed is back. The Edge deploy also deploys functions one at a time with no rollback (deploy.yml:138-159). A failure part-way through leaves new and old functions running side by side.
- **Failure scenario:** A PR adds a new RPC migration and the store code that calls it. On merge, Vercel publishes vyact.app in about a minute. `supabase db push` then fails, for example on an expired SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN, which happened for four straight releases (deploy.yml:45-48). Every user's write through the new RPC now fails with 'function does not exist' until someone notices. In the same way, an app that calls a new whatsapp-verify-otp action goes live while the function deploy is still queued behind migrations, or after it failed at `whatsapp-notify`.
- **Recommendation:** Make the app publish depend on the backend. Either (a) turn off Vercel auto-production for `main` and promote from a deploy.yml job with `needs: [automation, db-migrations, deploy-edge-functions]` (`vercel deploy --prebuilt --prod` with a token for the authen27 team), or (b) enable Vercel Deployment Checks that require the GitHub 'Deploy' and 'CI' checks before a deployment is promoted. Add a PR lint that fails when one PR changes both `supabase/migrations/**` and `react/src/**`, which enforces schema-first mechanically. Reopen TD-33. Proof: merge a PR whose migration deliberately fails and confirm vyact.app still serves the previous deployment.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): deploy.yml:21-24 triggers on push with no app job; its own comment at 58-63 and ENVIRONMENTS.md:22 say Vercel publishes regardless and stays live if the migration fails. ci.yml:120/161 limit E2E to PRs. Schema-first ordering is a convention only. One correction: the TD-33 'fix' never gated vyact.app, because the deleted consumer job deployed to the wrong team (deploy.yml:9-12). The gap is unchanged, not newly regres…

### OPS-06 · HIGH · No production observability: faults go nowhere, cron/pg_net results unchecked, no alerting

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** reliability · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit 10.4 (still open, now worse: the in-app transport is gone); TD-43
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/faults.ts:56-58`, `react/src/lib/faults.ts:84-99`, `react/src/components/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx:23-28`, `react/src/App.tsx:67`, `supabase/migrations/20260926120000_w2_whatsapp_preferences_dispatch.sql:140-158`, `supabase/migrations/20261003120000_web_push.sql:209-217`, `supabase/functions/send-split-email/index.ts:146-149`, `TECH_DEBT.md (TD-43)`
- **Evidence:** `setFaultTransport` has no caller outside tests, so `unexpected()` and `droppedWrite()`, 'the highest-stakes case: a user's write was dropped', end in the user's own browser console. The in-app SyncHealthIndicator transport the 2026-09 audit described was removed (App.tsx:67). ErrorBoundary.componentDidCatch holds only a commented-out Sentry placeholder and logs nothing. No Sentry or other error-tracking SDK appears anywhere in the code. Five pg_cron jobs call Edge functions through `net.http_post`, which is fire-and-forget. No code, migration or admin page reads `cron.job_run_details` or `net._http_response`, and nothing alerts on WhatsApp inbox `failed` rows or ai_usage errors. History: TD-43 records a two-day ask-vyact production outage that went unnoticed. v10.60.0 found that split email had never been configured and every call silently returned `email_not_configured`.
- **Failure scenario:** The Vault secret is rotated, or the web-push function is redeployed with a broken import. Every 15 minutes `web-push-dispatch` gets a 401 or 500, and no bill reminder or large-spend push reaches any device. In the browser, a sync fault drops a user's transaction write and records it only in that user's console. Both persist until a user complains, which is the same failure shape as the 2-day Pip outage.
- **Recommendation:** Register a privacy-scrubbed fault transport at app start: Sentry with beforeSend stripping amounts, descriptions and emails, or an insert-only `client_faults` RPC. Call it from ErrorBoundary.componentDidCatch. Add a pg_cron health job that checks for the last hour's failed `cron.job_run_details`, `net._http_response` status >= 400, `whatsapp_inbound_messages.status='failed'` older than N minutes, and ai_usage error ratio, and alerts the owner by email or WhatsApp template. Add an external uptime probe for vyact.app, /learn and an ask-vyact health endpoint, plus a post-deploy ask-vyact smoke using a test-user JWT (closes TD-43).
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): setFaultTransport (faults.ts:56) has no non-test caller, so unexpected() and droppedWrite() only reach console.error. ErrorBoundary.tsx:23-28 has only a commented Sentry placeholder. App.tsx:67 records the removal of SyncHealthIndicator. Admin Settings.tsx:43 shows Sentry as 'Not connected'. Nothing reads job_run_details or _http_response. Admin Intelligence shows ai_usage but raises no alerts. TD-43's unnoticed out…
- **Also reported as:** FE-PLT-09 (No production error telemetry: ErrorBoundary reports nothing and app_error_boundary is ne…)

### OPS-02 · MEDIUM · Migrations first run against production: no CI apply, no dry run, no automatic backup

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** ops-release · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit 10.1 / 10.2 (database migration validation); TD-02 (integration layer open)
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:58-114`, `scripts/db-migrations-check.mjs:10-24`, `scripts/db-migrations-check.mjs:109-134`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:101-104`, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:40-45`, `react/e2e/lane-b/env.ts:10-13`, `OPERATIONS.md:53-56`, `OPERATIONS.md:160`, `scripts/validate-new-migrations.mjs:1-12`, `supabase/migrations/20260926120000_w2_whatsapp_preferences_dispatch.sql:20`, `supabase/migrations/20260926120000_w2_whatsapp_preferences_dispatch.sql:131-158`
- **Evidence:** No CI job starts Postgres or Supabase and applies `supabase/migrations`. The only migration gate, db-migrations-check.mjs, checks file naming, duplicate timestamps, empty files and that db/schema.sql equals the concatenation; it never parses or executes SQL. deploy.yml goes straight to `supabase db push --password ... --include-all` against production, with no `--dry-run` and no backup step. vyact-test, the only cloud DB, is not migrated by any pipeline: 'Schema at parity with production as of 2026-09-28', and the nonprod pipeline is 'ON HOLD' (ENVIRONMENTS.md:40-45). env.ts:11 says Lane B's schema is 'applied from db/schema.sql' by hand. validate-new-migrations.mjs is a static regex checker that nothing runs. 54 of 87 migrations contain explicit BEGIN/COMMIT, and some run statements after their COMMIT: w2 COMMITs at line 131, then creates extensions and cron jobs (135-158). If one of those later statements fails, the CLI's per-file atomicity no longer holds. Rollback is 'promote the previous Vercel deployment and fix forward' (OPERATIONS.md:160), and a backup is a manual, optional pg_dump (OPERATIONS.md:53-56).
- **Failure scenario:** A migration that compiles fine but fails at runtime, for example an ALTER that a production row violates, a function body referencing a column renamed two migrations earlier, or a cron statement after COMMIT that errors. It is first executed by `db push` on dmxqkvploojokffuhxnz. With an explicit COMMIT mid-file, the tables and RPCs above the COMMIT are committed while the file is never recorded in schema_migrations. Production is left half-migrated, the next push re-runs the whole file, and there is no restore point.
- **Recommendation:** Add a PR job, triggered on `supabase/migrations/**`, that runs `supabase start` (or a postgres:15 container with the Supabase roles) and `supabase db reset` to apply all 87 migrations in order and then runs the PGlite and RLS smoke specs. Add a migration lint that rejects top-level BEGIN/COMMIT, or at least statements after COMMIT. In deploy.yml, take a logical backup (`supabase db dump --data-only` to an encrypted artifact) or confirm a PITR point before `db push`. Wire validate-new-migrations.mjs into automation-run, or delete it. Proof: a PR adding `select 1/0;` to a new migration must fail CI before merge.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): db-migrations-check.mjs:10-24 checks naming and the snapshot only. deploy.yml:104 runs `db push --include-all` on prod with no dry run and no backup. validate-new-migrations.mjs has no caller. In the w2 migration, COMMIT is at line 131 and cron and extension statements follow at 135-158. There is slight mitigation: loanPaymentSql.test.ts:46 runs some migrations in PGlite, and the statements are mostly idempotent (if…

### OPS-03 · MEDIUM · `db push --include-all` applies out-of-order migrations, so 'last filename wins' can be false in prod

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** data-integrity · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:24`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:41`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:65`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:104`, `scripts/db-migrations-check.mjs:55-66`, `OPERATIONS.md:44-49`
- **Evidence:** deploy.yml:104 runs `supabase db push ... --include-all`, which applies any local migration missing from the remote history, including ones whose timestamp is older than the latest applied. db-migrations-check.mjs only refuses duplicate timestamps (62-65). It does not require new files to sort after origin/main's newest. deploy.yml also has `workflow_dispatch` (24) and checks out whatever ref is chosen (65), so a manual run from a feature branch pushes that branch's unmerged migrations and functions to production. The only barrier is the `production-database` environment, and nothing in the repo shows a main-only branch rule on it.
- **Failure scenario:** Branch B is cut on 3 Oct and adds 20261003130000_fix.sql, which redefines `whatsapp_log_transaction`. Main merges 20261005120000_x.sql, which also redefines it, and deploys. B merges on 8 Oct, and --include-all applies B's file last. Production now runs B's body, while db/schema.sql, the repo and every 'audit the latest by filename' review (CLAUDE.md, this audit's own rule 3) read 20261005's body as live. The two disagree with nothing to say so.
- **Recommendation:** Add a gate that every migration added by a PR has a timestamp greater than the maximum on origin/main (fail with 'rename to <now>'). Remove `--include-all` once the baseline is confirmed in remote history, so the CLI errors on out-of-order files. Restrict the `production-database` environment to the `main` branch, or guard the job with `if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`. Proof: a fixture PR with an older-timestamped migration fails the gate.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): deploy.yml:104 runs `db push --include-all`. db-migrations-check.mjs:55-66 rejects only bad names and duplicate timestamps, with no check that new files sort after main's newest. workflow_dispatch (deploy.yml:24) plus checkout of the chosen ref (:65) let a non-main ref deploy. Timestamps are future-dated (20261007120000 on 5 Oct), which makes out-of-order merges likelier. Nothing in the repo restricts the environmen…

### OPS-04 · MEDIUM · send-split-email and delete-account are never deployed by the pipeline; v10.59/v10.60 fixes not shipped

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** ops-release · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit 10.1 (Edge manifest omission, still open)
- **Where:** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:138-159`, `supabase/functions/send-split-email/index.ts:49-51`, `supabase/functions/send-split-email/index.ts:101`, `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:1-6`, `react/CHANGELOG.md:40-42`, `react/src/lib/splitEmail.ts`, `VYACT_ENGINEERING_AUDIT_2026-09-07.md:1117`
- **Evidence:** supabase/functions has 10 deployable functions. deploy.yml deploys only 8: whatsapp-webhook, -send-otp, -verify-otp, -notify, -dispatch, web-push, ask-vyact and ask-vyact-relay-status. `send-split-email` and `delete-account` appear in no workflow and no current runbook, apart from docs/split-email-setup.md:66, which says to deploy by hand. Commit 13a5ac6 (v10.60.0) changed send-split-email/index.ts: the default sender is now `'Vyact <hello@vyact.app>'` instead of `onboarding@resend.dev`, reply_to was added, and provider refusals are now logged. The CHANGELOG (40-42) presents this as shipped. v10.59.0's invitation copy lives in `_shared/emailTemplates.ts`, which only reaches production when this function is redeployed. The 2026-09 audit flagged the same omission (line 1117).
- **Failure scenario:** v10.60.0 merges and Vercel ships the client that reads `{ok, reason, sent, recipients}`. The production send-split-email is still whatever build someone last deployed by hand, so it sends from onboarding@resend.dev, which is not authorised for vyact.app recipients, and logs nothing. That is the silent failure v10.60.0 says it fixed. A future privacy fix to delete-account, such as a new household-scoped table to erase, would equally never reach production.
- **Recommendation:** Deploy every function directory in a loop, as deploy-test-functions.yml already does, keeping a NO_JWT allowlist. Add a gate that fails when a directory under supabase/functions (excluding `_*`) is missing from the deploy manifest. Record the deployed function version or commit in the run summary. Proof: `supabase functions list` after a deploy shows every function updated to the same commit.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): deploy.yml:138-159 deploys 8 functions. send-split-email and delete-account exist in supabase/functions but appear in no prod workflow (only the vyact-test loop). 13a5ac6 changed send-split-email (DEFAULT_FROM, refusal logging, `recipients`). The old live build returns {ok:true,sent} with no recipients field, so readSplitEmailResult sees ok and a failure stays silent. The commit is still only on the branch, so this …

### OPS-05 · MEDIUM · Preview guard is skipped when VITE_SUPABASE_URL is 'All Environments'; admin previews use prod

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `react/vite.config.ts:17-21`, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:27-34`, `react/src/lib/supabase.ts:24-36`, `admin/src/lib/supabase.ts:14`, `admin/vite.config.ts:7-12`, `react/src/lib/__tests__/publicPages.test.ts:546-561`, `react/CHANGELOG.md:152-156`
- **Evidence:** The guard only fires when `process.env.VERCEL_ENV === 'preview' && !process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL`. ENVIRONMENTS.md:27 says production sets `VITE_SUPABASE_URL / VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY (All Environments)`. In Vercel that scope includes Preview, so every feature-branch preview receives the production URL, the guard is skipped, and the bundle connects to dmxqkvploojokffuhxnz. Only `staging` and `develop` have branch-scoped overrides to vyact-test. The v10.57.0 fix was checked with 'a local VERCEL_ENV=preview build', which has no injected env and so cannot reproduce this. PUB-026 only regex-matches the guard's source text. The admin app has no preview guard at all: its supabase.ts falls back to the production URL whenever `import.meta.env.PROD`, and `admin/.env.production` is committed.
- **Failure scenario:** A team member opens the Vercel preview of an unreviewed branch, such as a refactor of the sync outbox or a migration-dependent form, and signs in. The experimental code then writes to production households: for example, a buggy patch that drops `opening_balance` on an account edit corrupts a real balance. CLAUDE.md's binding claim that 'A Vercel preview never touches production data' is false. Admin previews give unreviewed super-admin UI code full production access.
- **Recommendation:** Turn the guard around and fail closed. When VERCEL_ENV==='preview', allow cloud mode only if VITE_SUPABASE_URL's host is the test ref xdbfzwltqocyljbgztmv; otherwise force local-only. Add a post-build assertion that greps dist for `dmxqkvploojokffuhxnz` and fails a preview build that contains it, and do the same for admin. Rescope the Vercel production env vars to Production only. Confirm today's exposure by fetching a feature-branch preview's index-*.js and grepping for the production ref. Replace PUB-026's source regex with a test that runs the build under VERCEL_ENV=preview plus an injected production URL.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): vite.config.ts:17 skips the guard whenever VITE_SUPABASE_URL is injected, and ENVIRONMENTS.md:27 documents that value as 'All Environments'. That last point is a Vercel setting the repo cannot confirm. Admin has no guard: admin/src/lib/supabase.ts:14 uses the prod fallback, and admin/.env.production is committed with the prod URL. Mitigations: previews need a Vercel team login (ENVIRONMENTS.md:16), and RLS limits wr…

### OPS-08 · MEDIUM · RLS isolation covers 3 of ~40 tables, PR-only, against a hand-synced schema; INV-7 still untested

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** testing · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** TD-19 (Lane B partially delivered); TD-31 (open); 2026-09 audit 10.2
- **Where:** `react/e2e/lane-b/rls-isolation.spec.ts:53-110`, `react/e2e/lane-b/env.ts:10-13`, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:40-41`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:159-203`, `react/playwright.lane-b.config.ts:39`, `scripts/test-inventory-config.mjs:36`
- **Evidence:** The negative-isolation specs only touch `transactions`, `households` and `memberships` (rls-isolation.spec.ts:57-110). Migrations enable RLS on about 40 tables (accounts, budgets, budget_allocations, debts, assets, recurring_schedules, shared_splits/shares, net_worth_snapshots, push_subscriptions, whatsapp_*, ai_usage, ai_feedback, agent_*). Migrations also contain 107 `security definer` occurrences. No cross-household test covers any RPC (upsert_budget, delete_account, move_account_and_delete, whatsapp_* writers, record_net_worth_snapshot). Lane B runs only on pull_request, against vyact-test, whose schema is applied by hand ('parity ... as of 2026-09-28'). Nine production migrations have landed since, including the HOUSEHOLD_FULL triggers. The only SQL-integration layer is a focused PGlite fixture for loan payments. TD-31 (INV-7 atomicity) is still unimplemented. Lane B's report goes to `playwright-report-lane-b` (config:39), but CI uploads `react/playwright-report` (ci.yml:202), so Lane B evidence is never kept.
- **Failure scenario:** A new SECURITY DEFINER RPC (for example a v10.59 split-sharing helper) takes a `p_household_id` and forgets the `is_member()` check. Alice calls it with Bob's household id and reads or writes Bob's accounts. Unit tests mock the transport, Lane A has no DB, and Lane B never touches that table or RPC, so CI stays green.
- **Recommendation:** Generate isolation specs from a table and RPC manifest. For every RLS table, user B gets select, update and delete returning 0 rows or an error on user A's row. For every SECURITY DEFINER RPC that takes a household or account id, user B is refused. Run them on a `supabase db reset` database built from the current migrations (see the migrations finding) instead of hand-synced vyact-test. Add the INV-7 force-fail test (TD-31) there. Fix the upload path to `react/playwright-report-lane-b`.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Core holds: rls-isolation.spec.ts touches only transactions, households and memberships; no lane-b spec tests cross-household RPCs; Lane B is PR-only (ci.yml:160). The report folder mismatch is real (playwright.lane-b.config.ts:39 vs ci.yml:202). Overstated: write-not-applied.spec.ts:33-110 also covers cross-household writes on recurring_schedules and households, so coverage is 4 tables, not 3.

### OPS-11 · MEDIUM · LLM spend guard is per-user only and fails open if the cap env is unset or invalid

- **Slice:** CI/CD, release, environments, observability, test strategy · **Category:** cost · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** 2026-09 audit A2 (atomicity fixed; global ceiling and fail-closed config not addressed)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/ask-vyact/index.ts:413-440`, `supabase/migrations (reserve_ai_usage, latest definition, lines 31-64 of the A2 migration)`
- **Evidence:** The gateway reads `const dailyCap = Number.parseInt(env('ASK_VYACT_DAILY_CALL_CAP', '200'), 10)` and reserves only `if (Number.isFinite(dailyCap) && dailyCap > 0)`. A value of '0', a negative number or a non-numeric typo silently disables the cap instead of refusing calls. reserve_ai_usage counts per `user_id` over 24h. There is no household-, global- or money-denominated ceiling, and no alert on ai_usage cost (see the observability finding). Sign-up is open.
- **Failure scenario:** An operator sets ASK_VYACT_DAILY_CALL_CAP='unlimited' or '0' meaning 'no limit for testing', and every user now has no cap. Even when correctly set, 100 scripted free accounts × 200 calls per day = 20,000 provider calls per day, and nothing stops or reports the spend until the provider invoice arrives.
- **Recommendation:** Fail closed: if the cap env does not parse to a positive integer, refuse with 503 `quota_misconfigured`. Add a global daily ceiling in reserve_ai_usage, counted under an advisory lock on a single row, or a sum of `ai_usage.cost` over 24h against a configured budget. Alert the owner at 50% and 80%. Proof: a gatewayWorkflow test with cap='abc' expects 503 and no provider call.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): ask-vyact/index.ts:419-421 skips the reservation when the cap is NaN or ≤0, and whatsapp-webhook:1086 does the same, so a typo fails open. However '0 = off' is a documented intent (index.ts:84), not a misconfiguration. reserve_ai_usage (20260909120100:31-64) counts per user only; no global or cost ceiling exists, and sign-up is open.

### XC-PRV-01 · CRITICAL · Production Supabase secret API key committed in git history (setup.sh) while the repo was public

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** critical · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `setup.sh@4df29b7:11 (added 2026-05-10)`, `setup.sh@7878940 (deleted 2026-06-01; blob still reachable in history)`, `vyact-master-todo.md:130-131`, `SECURITY.md:42`
- **Evidence:** `git log --all -G'sb_secret_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}'` returns 4df29b7 (adds setup.sh) and 7878940 (removes it). In 4df29b7:setup.sh line 11 is a `SECRET_KEY=` assignment holding an `sb_secret_` key (Supabase secret API key, service-role equivalent; value not reproduced). The same file names the live project (`SUPABASE_PROJECT_ID="dmxqkvploojokffuhxnz"`, line 8) and sends the key as `Authorization: Bearer ${SECRET_KEY}` (line ~29). The key was only removed from the tip when the vanilla shell was archived (VERSIONS.md:115 lists setup.sh among archived files). vyact-master-todo.md:130 says the repo was made private on 30 Sep 2026, so it was public for about 4 months with this key in history. I found no rotation or revocation record in CHANGELOG, VERSIONS, docs, TECH_DEBT or SECURITY.md. SECURITY.md:42 says the service-role key never ships, but it was committed. Related open item: vyact-master-todo.md:131 (#2) says an exposed Google OAuth client secret is still 'Open', not rotated. I found no GOCSPX- value in the tree or history. No secret scanner runs in .github/workflows (ci.yml, deploy.yml, deploy-test-functions.yml, lighthouse.yml). Other scans were clean: no JWTs in the tree, builds, reports or history; no AIza/sk-/EAA/ghp_/re_/sbp_ keys in tracked content; the postgres:// URLs in OPERATIONS.md are placeholders.
- **Failure scenario:** Anyone who cloned or scraped the public repo between May and Sep 2026 can recover the key with `git show 4df29b7:setup.sh`. If the key was never revoked, it bypasses RLS on project dmxqkvploojokffuhxnz: read and write every household's ledger, auth.users emails, whatsapp_identities phone numbers and WhatsApp message payloads, plus Admin-API user deletion. That is complete cross-tenant compromise.
- **Recommendation:** Today, in the Supabase dashboard (Project Settings, API Keys), revoke that specific sb_secret_ key and issue a new one. Update every consumer: Edge Function secrets and any CI secret. Review Supabase API and auth logs since 2026-05-10 for requests made with the old key. Rotate the Google OAuth secret (todo #2) in the same session. Then rewrite history with git filter-repo to drop setup.sh and vercel-setup.sh, force-push, and ask GitHub support to purge cached views. Add gitleaks or trufflehog as a CI step on every PR and a pre-commit hook. Proof: a gitleaks run over full history exits 0, and a request with the old key returns 401.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): `git show 4df29b7:setup.sh` line 11 holds a live-format `SECRET_KEY="sb_secret_..."` for project dmxqkvploojokffuhxnz (line 8), used as a Bearer token (line ~29). 7878940 removed it from the tip only. No rotation record exists in any tracked doc. vyact-master-todo.md (untracked) records the repo going private on 30 Sep. I cannot check from the repo whether the key was revoked.

### XC-PRV-03 · HIGH · Session replay (Contentsquare) and Userback screenshots run on every finance screen, undisclosed, no consent

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** compliance · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/index.html:35-75`, `react/src/main.tsx:4-5`, `react/src/main.tsx:27`, `react/src/main.tsx:39`, `react/src/main.tsx:46`, `react/CHANGELOG.md:6001-6006`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:89-96`, `react/src/pages/Cookies.tsx:13-18`, `react/src/pages/Cookies.tsx:80`, `react/vercel.json:96-111`, `Userback/Recurring Module should be household + user specific.png`
- **Evidence:** index.html injects `https://t.contentsquare.net/uxa/86f8fcfc0d114.js` on vyact.app on every route except a cold '/' (`if (!onLanding) contentsquare();`). On '/' it loads on the first interaction and keeps running as the SPA navigates into the app. The changelog entry that added it (CHANGELOG.md:6001-6006) says it 'Captures ... mouse movement trails, and session video'. No `data-cs-mask` or other masking attribute exists anywhere in react/src. main.tsx starts the Userback widget (screenshot plus annotation) on every host, with no host gate, so it also runs in the demo and on localhost. It also mounts `<SpeedInsights/>`. The tracked file Userback/*.png shows a submitted report with the reporter's email and a Salary ₹1,00,009 and Rent screen. GA4 also loads unconditionally on vyact.app: there is no consent banner, no Consent Mode and no opt-out (grep finds no consent UI). Privacy §5 says data is shared 'only with' Supabase, Vercel, Meta and LLM providers. Contentsquare, Userback and Google Analytics are not named. The Cookies table lists only GA4 'if enabled for your environment' and says analytics are 'optional', but the only control offered (line 80) is the browser's own settings. No Content-Security-Policy header is set (vercel.json sets nosniff, X-Frame-Options, Referrer-Policy and Permissions-Policy only), so these third-party scripts have full DOM and storage access.
- **Failure scenario:** An EU or Indian user signs in and opens Net Worth or Transactions. Contentsquare records a replay of the rendered DOM, including balances, account and asset names, transaction descriptions and household member names, and sends it to Contentsquare without the user's consent and without disclosure. Under GDPR/ePrivacy, non-essential trackers need prior opt-in, and DPDP s.5-6 requires an itemised notice and consent. Both are missing, and the published Privacy Policy is false about who receives data.
- **Recommendation:** Remove Contentsquare from authenticated routes, or gate it and GA4 behind an explicit consent banner (Consent Mode v2 defaults to denied). Configure Contentsquare masking for all text nodes in the app (data-cs-mask on the app root), or limit it to the public pages. Give Userback the same host gate as GA and disable its auto-screenshot on finance routes. List every processor (Google Analytics, Contentsquare, Userback, Vercel Speed Insights, email provider, OpenRouter) in Privacy §5 and the Cookie table. Add a CSP restricting script-src. Test: a publicPages test asserting that neither t.contentsquare.net nor Userback is requested on /dashboard before consent is stored.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): index.html:60-63 loads Contentsquare on vyact.app for every non-landing route, which includes the authenticated finance screens. No data-cs-mask exists anywhere in react/src. main.tsx:27/39 starts Userback on every host. There is no consent UI. Privacy.tsx:89-96 lists processors 'only' as Supabase, Vercel, Meta and LLM providers, with no GA, Contentsquare or Userback. Cookies.tsx:80 offers browser settings as the on…
- **Also reported as:** FE-UIS-05 (Session replay (Contentsquare/Hotjar) and Userback run in the signed-in finance app, undi…); FE-PLT-03 (Session-replay and analytics scripts run on authenticated financial screens without conse…)

### XC-ARC-06 · MEDIUM · Bundled engine left hand ports live: server money, recurring and budget alerts are duplicated

- **Slice:** Architecture, maintainability and AI-assisted development risk · **Category:** architecture · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/engine.generated.js:4-36`, `scripts/build-agent-engine.mjs:1-12`, `supabase/functions/_shared/money/calculations.ts:1-33`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:17`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts:126-160`, `react/src/lib/notifications.ts:183-211`, `supabase/functions/_shared/recurring.ts:1-20`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:1-6`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-parser.ts:72-77`, `CLAUDE.md:378`
- **Evidence:** W4 (v10.45.0) solved 'edge cannot import react/src'. build-agent-engine.mjs bundles the app's own calculations.ts, money.ts (dinero), recurring.ts, askVyactParser.ts, constants.ts, notifications.ts and supabaseAdapter.ts into engine.generated.js (module list in its header, :4-36), with a --check gate and a purity guard. The older hand ports were never retired: `_shared/money/calculations.ts` (814 lines, which reimplements dinero 'in plain integer minor-unit arithmetic') is still the money source for the WhatsApp scheduler (dispatch-rules.ts:17); `_shared/recurring.ts` (136 lines) still drives 'paid X'; `whatsapp-parser.ts` still ports askVyactParser ('kept self-contained because edge deploy can't import from react/src', :4-5; 'keep the two identical', :72). The same business rule exists twice with different behaviour. The push budget alert (notifications.ts:183-211) fires at 80% and at 100%, converts currency with ctx.rates, and handles legacy single-category budgets. The WhatsApp alert (dispatch-rules.ts:126-160) skips at or above 100%, returns nothing for a non-INR household or one with any foreign-currency transaction, ignores budgets without allocations, and floors the percentage. The edge runtime therefore holds two dinero implementations at once.
- **Failure scenario:** A household with one USD transaction among its INR rows crosses 80% of its Groceries line. Web push (bundled engine) notifies; WhatsApp (hand port) silently sends nothing. At 100% push says 'at 100%' and WhatsApp stays silent. Any future fix to calculations.ts (for example the convert-missing-rate-as-1 footgun) lands in the app, Pip and push, but not in the scheduler's figures until someone remembers to edit the port. Parity tests pin only today's fixtures.
- **Recommendation:** Export spendByCategoryInRange, reportableTxns, effectiveAmount, recurringInstanceId, computeNext… and parseWhatsAppMessage (built on askVyactParser) from serverEngine.ts, and switch whatsapp-dispatch-rules.ts, whatsapp-webhook and _shared/recurring.ts to engine.ts. Delete _shared/money/calculations.ts, _shared/recurring.ts and most of whatsapp-parser.ts, and keep moneyPortParity only for what remains. Generate the one remaining Deno allowlist from constants.ts in the build script, which collapses the 'three places' category and payment-mode rule to one. Have one budget-threshold rule feed both channels, with channel-specific suppression as a separate policy step.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): The hand ports really are live. dispatch-rules.ts:17 imports _shared/money/calculations.ts (814 lines, reimplements dinero), and whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:43 imports _shared/recurring.ts, alongside engine.generated.js. The claim is overstated in two ways. Parity tests gate both ports (moneyPortParity.test.ts, recurringPort.test.ts). The WhatsApp skips at 100% and on foreign currency are deliberate, documented policy…
- **Also reported as:** INT-PAR-07 (Hand-ported money port and label tables still live after W4, untyped and drifting)

### XC-ARC-11 · MEDIUM · The 'never a silent write-loss catch {}' rule has no enforcement: 110 empty catches, 7 on writes

- **Slice:** Architecture, maintainability and AI-assisted development risk · **Category:** maintainability · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial) · **Known:** TD-24 (marked resolved; rule not enforced)
- **Where:** `CLAUDE.md:156-157`, `react/eslint.config.js:45-55`, `react/src/store/slices/sharedSplitsSlice.ts:106-113`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:133`, `react/src/store/slices/recurringSlice.ts:162`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:264`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:282`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:462`, `react/src/store/slices/dataSlice.ts:710`
- **Evidence:** CLAUDE.md:156 makes it binding: faults go through lib/faults.ts and there must 'never [be] a silent write-loss catch {} on a write/contract path'. ESLint has no rule against it (`no-empty` is not configured; only rules-of-hooks is an error). A grep finds 110 `catch { /* … */ }` blocks in react/src. Write-path examples: deleteSharedSplitForTxn does `try { await deleteSharedSplit(existing.id); } catch { /* best-effort */ }` and then removes the split from local state regardless (sharedSplitsSlice.ts:111-112). This is a direct Supabase call, not a queued one. The recurring engine persists the advanced schedule with `catch { /* best-effort */ }` (recurringSlice.ts:133, :162). There are also an init-time recurring upsert (dataSlice.ts:264), ensureDefaultCashAccount (:282, :462) and an educationProgress cloud write (:710).
- **Failure scenario:** An owner deletes a split transaction while offline or during a 5xx. The shared_splits row stays on the server, the owner's UI shows it gone, and every participant keeps seeing 'you owe ₹1,250' for an expense that no longer exists. Nothing records a fault, so support cannot see it.
- **Recommendation:** Route these catches through `unexpected(e, 'context')` from lib/faults.ts. For deleteSharedSplitForTxn, keep local state until the server confirms, or queue the delete. Enable `no-empty: ['error', { allowEmptyCatch: false }]` together with a small allowlist via eslint-disable plus a reason, so the rule is mechanical. Add a test that makes deleteSharedSplit reject and asserts the split is still listed and a fault is recorded.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Real defect: sharedSplitsSlice.ts:111 swallows a direct Supabase delete (sharedSplits.ts:222 throws) and drops the split from local state anyway, so participants keep seeing it. Overstated: the recurring catches (recurringSlice.ts:133,:162) and the dataSlice.ts:264 upsert go through HybridAdapter.upsert, which queues to the durable outbox (hybridAdapter.ts:248-255), so a network failure does not lose them. Also, no-…
- **Also reported as:** FE-SYN-09 (Write paths still swallow failures, breaking the binding no-silent-write-loss rule)

### XC-PRV-02 · MEDIUM · Recipient email and household-invite bearer tokens sent to GA4/Contentsquare via page URLs

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `supabase/functions/_shared/emailTemplates.ts:94`, `react/src/pages/auth/SignUp.tsx:20-21`, `react/index.html:51-55`, `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:533`, `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:710`, `react/src/pages/auth/AcceptInvite.tsx:10-12`, `react/src/pages/auth/AcceptInvite.tsx:54`, `supabase/migrations/20260601153000_fix_shareable_invite_accept_rpc.sql:13-54`, `docs/measurement/events.yaml:11`
- **Evidence:** The split-invite email links to `${d.appUrl}/auth/sign-up?email=${encodeURIComponent(d.recipientEmail)}&method=email&next=%2Fsplits&ref=split_invite` (emailTemplates.ts:94). SignUp.tsx:21 reads `params.get('email')` and never strips it from the URL. index.html:55 runs `gtag('config','G-E3XKWZP850')` with default settings, so GA4 sends a page_view whose page_location is the full URL, query string included, before React mounts. Household invites are path bearer tokens (`/invite/${encodeURIComponent(inv.token)}`, Households.tsx:533 and 710). A signed-out visitor is redirected with `next=/invite/<token>` (AcceptInvite.tsx:54). `accept_invitation_link(invite_token)` admits any signed-in caller who presents a valid token; it does not check the email (migration lines 13-54). Contentsquare and Userback load on these same routes (index.html:59-66, main.tsx:39). events.yaml:11 says 'PII is forbidden', and CLAUDE.md says funnel events are never sent with personal data.
- **Failure scenario:** Alice shares a split with bob@example.org, who has no account. Bob opens the email and GA4 records page_location `https://vyact.app/auth/sign-up?email=bob%40example.org&...` against the Vyact property, where Google and anyone with GA access can read it. This breaches Google Analytics' no-PII terms and the stated policy. The owner of a Family household copies an invite link and the invitee opens it signed out. The token is now stored in GA (`/invite/<token>` and `/auth/sign-in?next=%2Finvite%2F<token>`) and in Contentsquare's recordings until the invite expires. Anyone with access to either tool who signs in with any account can join that household and read its finances.
- **Recommendation:** Configure gtag with a sanitised location: `gtag('config', id, { page_location: location.origin + location.pathname.replace(/^\/invite\/.+/, '/invite/:token') })` and drop known query keys (email, next, code, token). Better still, put the invite token in the URL fragment and strip it with history.replaceState before any analytics loads. Replace `?email=` in the split-invite link with an opaque signed reference that the server resolves. Do the same for Contentsquare's URL masking. Add a unit test that loads index.html's snippet with `/auth/sign-up?email=a@b.c` and `/invite/abc` and asserts that no dataLayer entry contains '@' or the token.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): The leak is real. emailTemplates.ts:94 puts ?email= in the link, and index.html:55 has a default gtag config that records the full page_location. AcceptInvite.tsx:54 puts the token in next=. It is overstated: accept_invitation_link sets accepted_at (migration :54), so a token is single-use and dies once the invitee accepts. GA4's default email redaction may also strip the email. Only someone with GA or Contentsquare…
- **Also reported as:** INT-HH-05 (Household invite bearer token in URL path is sent to GA4 and Contentsquare)

### XC-PRV-04 · MEDIUM · Email-code re-auth for erase/delete is client-side only; any session can wipe data immediately

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** security · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/pages/Settings.tsx:182-213`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:257-260`, `react/src/lib/auth.ts:263-283`, `supabase/functions/delete-account/index.ts:44-64`, `supabase/migrations/20260911150000_v1030_net_worth_snapshots.sql:119-158`, `supabase/migrations/20260701120000_v98_privacy_deletion_controls.sql:116-134`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:152`
- **Evidence:** Privacy §7 (line 152) says every Danger Zone action 'requires entering a one-time verification code ... proof the request really is coming from you, not just a signed-in browser'. That check is only `confirmWithEmailCode(...)` in Settings.tsx (lines 190 and 206), and nothing on the server verifies it. `request_account_deletion()` only updates the caller's profile. `delete-account` checks only `admin.auth.getUser(jwt)` and `deletion_requested_at`, then honours a client-supplied `{ immediate: true }` (lines 48, 62). It does not check session age, `amr` or an OTP nonce. `erase_household_data(h_id)` checks only that the caller's role is owner or admin.
- **Failure scenario:** Someone holding a user's access token can call `rpc('request_account_deletion')` and then `functions.invoke('delete-account', {body:{immediate:true}})`. That could be an unattended unlocked phone, a malicious browser extension, or any script injected through the third-party tags above, since there is no CSP. Every household the user owns, with all members' data, is erased irreversibly within seconds and no code is ever entered. A household admin's token can likewise erase the whole household through `erase_household_data`.
- **Recommendation:** Enforce re-authentication on the server. Have `verifyAccountActionCode` hit an Edge endpoint that records a short-lived (5 min) single-use 'danger nonce' bound to user_id, and have `delete-account` and `erase_household_data` (via a wrapper) require and consume it. Alternatively require the JWT's `amr` to contain otp/mfa with `iat` within 5 minutes. Integration test: calling delete-account with a valid but stale JWT and no nonce returns 403, and no rows are deleted.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): The OTP is checked only on the client (Settings.tsx:190,206; auth.ts verifyOtp). delete-account/index.ts:44-64 checks getUser(jwt) plus deletion_requested_at and honours a client-sent immediate:true. erase_household_data (20260911150000:128-135) checks only the caller's role. Privacy.tsx:152 overstates this. I lowered it because an owner's stolen token can already delete rows through ordinary RLS writes, so the miss…
- **Also reported as:** FE-PLT-06 (Email-code re-auth for deletion and erasure, and password change, are enforced only in th…); BE-FN-03 (Irreversible delete and erase rely on a client-only email-code check, so a stolen session…)

### XC-PRV-07 · MEDIUM · WhatsApp message content and phone numbers are retained forever and survive unlink, erase and deletion

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** high · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260614120000_whatsapp_connection_foundation.sql (create table whatsapp_inbound_messages: profile_id/household_id `on delete set null`, payload jsonb)`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-webhook/index.ts:194-206`, `supabase/functions/_shared/whatsapp-send.ts:90-103`, `supabase/functions/whatsapp-verify-otp/index.ts:59-64`, `supabase/migrations/20260911150000_v1030_net_worth_snapshots.sql:119-158`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:179`
- **Evidence:** The webhook upserts every inbound message with `payload: message`, the raw Meta object: sender phone (`from`) and body text such as 'spent 450 on groceries'. It does this even when `profile_id` is null, i.e. for numbers that were never linked. Outbound sends store `payload: { event, templateName, params: cleaned, ... }`, which includes first names and amounts. Both FKs are `on delete set null`, so deleting a profile or household orphans the rows instead of removing them. The WhatsApp unlink action deletes only OTPs and the identity (verify-otp:60-62). `erase_household_data` does not touch this table. No migration or function ever deletes from whatsapp_inbound_messages by age; the only cron jobs are dispatch and web-push. Privacy §9 says security logs are kept 'up to 12 months ... then deleted or anonymized'.
- **Failure scenario:** A user links WhatsApp, logs 18 months of spending by chat, then unlinks and deletes their account. Every message they ever sent, with their phone number and amounts, plus every digest sent to them, remains in production indefinitely with `profile_id = NULL`. A stranger who texts the business number has their number and text stored forever too. This breaks the erasure promise and DPDP storage limitation (s.8(7)).
- **Recommendation:** Change the FKs to `on delete cascade`. Delete the person's rows (`where profile_id = X`) in unlink, in the deletion purge and in erase_household_data (`where household_id = h_id`). Add a pg_cron retention job that nulls `payload` after the replay window (e.g. 30 days; status must be 'done' or 'failed' with attempts >= 3) and hard-deletes rows older than 12 months. Delete unlinked-sender rows after processing. Test: an SQL integration test that inserts inbound rows, runs unlink and then the purge, and asserts count = 0.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): whatsapp_inbound_messages (20260614120000:36-44) stores the raw payload, and its profile and household FKs are `on delete set null`. The webhook (:198-206) stores messages from unlinked senders too, and whatsapp-send.ts:93-103 stores outbound params. Unlink (verify-otp:59-63) and erase_household_data never delete these rows, and no retention job exists. I lowered it to medium because WhatsApp number linking is not y…
- **Also reported as:** BE-WA-10 (Raw WhatsApp messages and phone numbers are kept forever and orphaned after account delet…); BE-FN-05 (Account erasure leaves WhatsApp message bodies, phone numbers, agent raw inputs and push …); DB-OPS-06 (No retention or erasure for PII-bearing operational tables; WhatsApp text outlives deleti…)
