### XC-PRV-09 · MEDIUM · Consent record is unreliable: wrong version stored, errors swallowed, Google sign-in skips it

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** compliance · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `react/src/lib/auth.ts:199-209`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:3-9`, `react/src/pages/auth/SignUp.tsx:37-59`, `react/src/components/auth/AuthGate.tsx:69-73`, `react/src/pages/auth/SignIn.tsx:69`, `react/src/components/auth/GoogleButton.tsx:23-32`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:51-55`
- **Evidence:** Privacy.tsx:9 `POLICY_VERSION = '2026-09-07'`, whose comment says the value is 'recorded against an account at sign-up, so it is the auditable record of WHICH text a user accepted'. But `acceptPolicies` writes `tos_version`/`privacy_version: POLICY_ACCEPTANCE_VERSION` = '2026-07-01' (auth.ts:199-206), the text from before the LLM disclosure. Every call is `.catch(() => {})` (SignUp.tsx:48 and 59, AuthGate.tsx:71), so a failed write is silent. The fallback flag `pending_policy_accept` lives in sessionStorage, so it is lost when the email-verification link opens a new tab. SignIn.tsx:69 renders `<GoogleButton />` without `requireAgreement`. A first-time Google user who starts from Sign in gets an account with no acceptance shown or recorded. No re-consent flow exists when POLICY_VERSION changes, although Privacy §13 and Terms §12 promise one where required.
- **Failure scenario:** A user signs up on 1 Oct. Their profile records privacy_version '2026-07-01', which says nothing about questions being sent to an LLM provider. In a dispute or regulator inquiry, Vyact's own record shows consent to a text that did not cover the processing. A new user who taps 'Continue with Google' on the Sign in page has tos_accepted_at NULL.
- **Recommendation:** Export one constant, and have acceptPolicies write POLICY_VERSION. Record acceptance server-side: either a `handle_new_user` trigger reading `raw_user_meta_data.policy_version` passed in signUp options and in the OAuth `queryParams`, or block the app shell when `privacy_version <> POLICY_VERSION` and show a re-accept screen. Remove the silent catches and report through faults.ts. Unit test: acceptPolicies writes the same value Privacy renders, and AuthGate shows the re-accept screen for a stale version.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): auth.ts:199-206 writes POLICY_ACCEPTANCE_VERSION '2026-07-01' while Privacy.tsx:9 POLICY_VERSION='2026-09-07' claims to be the recorded version. All acceptPolicies calls .catch(()=>{}) (SignUp.tsx:48,59; AuthGate.tsx:71). Flag in sessionStorage. SignIn.tsx:69 renders <GoogleButton/> without requireAgreement and no terms text, so a first-time Google user gets no acceptance recorded.

### XC-PRV-10 · MEDIUM · Pip questions and figures routed through OpenRouter with no provider data policy enforced

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260906140000_seed_model_catalogue.sql:29-49`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/router.ts:204-209`, `supabase/functions/_shared/agent/router.ts:469-480`, `react/src/lib/askVyactLlm.ts:434-441`, `react/src/pages/Terms.tsx:61`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:95`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:104-111`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:187-189`
- **Evidence:** Every catalogue row uses `provider 'openrouter'`, `base_url https://openrouter.ai/api`, with upstream models from Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Meta, Qwen and DeepSeek. The request body is `{ model, messages, max_tokens, stream }` plus whitelisted `EXTRA_KEYS` (stop, penalties, seed, response_format, tool_choice, top_k, repetition_penalty). OpenRouter's `provider` routing object (data_collection:'deny', zdr, order, allow_fallbacks) cannot be set even from a config row, so whichever upstream host OpenRouter picks receives the question plus `facts`. Per CLAUDE.md v10.39, `facts` includes account and asset names with balances. No repo document records the OpenRouter account privacy settings. Terms §4 promises Your Content is 'never [used] to train third-party models'. Privacy names neither OpenRouter nor its upstream providers or regions (§5, §11), and says 'Exactly two things leave Vyact', without mentioning account and asset names.
- **Failure scenario:** The owner enables `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash` or `qwen/...` for cost. OpenRouter routes the prompt to an upstream that logs or trains on prompts, because the account default may allow it and the request does not forbid it. A user's question 'can I afford a ₹40,000 trip?' and their liquid_by_source breakdown (account names and balances) go to an undisclosed processor in an undisclosed jurisdiction, contradicting the Terms.
- **Recommendation:** Add `provider` to EXTRA_KEYS, or better, always inject `provider: { data_collection: 'deny', allow_fallbacks: false }` for base_url openrouter.ai in router.ts. Document the account-level ZDR setting in SUPABASE_CONFIG.md. Restrict the catalogue to providers with no-training terms. Name OpenRouter and the active upstream in Privacy §5, and amend 'exactly two things' to mention account and asset names. Router unit test: an openrouter config produces a body with provider.data_collection === 'deny'.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Confirmed: all seeded rows are openrouter (seed_model_catalogue.sql:29-49) and EXTRA_KEYS (router.ts:206-209) cannot carry OpenRouter's `provider` data-policy object; Privacy §5 (:90-95) never names OpenRouter. Overstated: seed rows are enabled=false, and OpenRouter account-level privacy settings (not visible in repo) may already deny training providers. Privacy :106 does mention names in answers.

### XC-PRV-11 · MEDIUM · Privacy notice lacks DPDP/GDPR basics: controller, grievance contact, breach process, child accounts

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** compliance · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed-with-caveats (adversarial: partial)
- **Where:** `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:43-56`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:138-148`, `react/src/pages/Privacy.tsx:187-193`, `react/src/pages/Terms.tsx:53-55`, `react/src/pages/Terms.tsx:95-97`, `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:470`, `react/src/pages/Households.tsx:736`, `supabase/functions/send-split-email/index.ts:100-105`, `SECURITY.md`
- **Evidence:** No operating legal entity or address is named; Terms §11 refers only to 'the jurisdiction in which the operating legal entity is incorporated'. No Data Protection Officer or grievance officer is named, which DPDP s.8(10)/s.13 requires; only support@vyact.app. Withdrawal of consent is 'contact us' (Privacy §6), while DPDP s.6(4) requires withdrawal to be as easy as giving consent. There is no breach-notification or incident procedure in SECURITY.md or docs; grep for breach/incident finds none. The processor list omits the email providers the code actually uses (MailerSend, SMTP and Resend in send-split-email:100-105), push services, analytics and replay vendors, and OpenRouter. International transfers (§11) are generic, with no Supabase region stated. Children: Privacy §12 says a 'child' member 'is not a separate account holder', but Households.tsx offers `<option value="child">Child (own data only)</option>` as an invitation role that the invitee accepts with their own account. Terms set the age at 16, but DPDP defines a child as under 18 and requires verifiable parental consent (s.9). No age is captured.
- **Failure scenario:** An Indian household invites its 15-year-old as 'Child'. The teenager creates an account, ticks the 16+ terms, and their personal data is processed with no verifiable parental consent, a DPDP s.9 violation. If a breach occurs, there is no documented process to notify the Data Protection Board and affected users.
- **Recommendation:** Add to Privacy: controller identity, a grievance officer and DPO contact, a DPDP section (rights, nomination, the Board's complaint route), a complete processor table with regions, and in-app consent withdrawal for each optional processing. Add an incident and breach runbook (72-hour GDPR, DPDP Board intimation) to SECURITY.md. For 'child' invites, require the inviting guardian's attestation, record it, and capture the date of birth at acceptance. Bump POLICY_VERSION and re-consent as in the consent finding.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (partial): Gaps are real: no controller/entity (Terms.tsx:96), no grievance contact, withdrawal is 'contact us' (Privacy.tsx:146), §11 generic, processor list omits email/OpenRouter (send-split-email:100-104), child invite role with own account (Households.tsx:736) contradicts Privacy §12. Overstated: DPO duty is for significant fiduciaries only and DPDP Rules obligations are still phasing in; legal-text gaps, not exploitable …

### XC-PRV-12 · MEDIUM · resolve_participant_names lets any signed-up user bulk-test emails and learn real names

- **Slice:** Secrets, PII and privacy compliance · **Category:** privacy · **Auditor severity:** medium · **Verification:** confirmed (adversarial: confirmed)
- **Where:** `supabase/migrations/20260802120000_split_participant_name_resolution.sql:12-30`, `react/src/lib/sharedSplits.ts:44-47`, `react/src/components/splits/SplitFormModal.tsx:227`, `react/src/components/splits/SplitFormModal.tsx:273`
- **Evidence:** `resolve_participant_names(p_emails text[])` is SECURITY DEFINER over `auth.users join profiles` and returns `(lower(u.email), p.display_name)` for every matching active account. It is granted to `authenticated`, and sign-up is open. The array has no length cap, the function does not tie the emails to the caller's own splits or households, and there is no rate limit. The migration comment acknowledges it as a 'pure directory lookup' with 'no auth.uid() gate'.
- **Failure scenario:** An attacker creates a free account and calls `rpc('resolve_participant_names', { p_emails: [...50,000 leaked emails] })`. They get back which addresses use a family-finance app and each person's display name. That is account enumeration plus disclosure of a sensitive relationship: being a Vyact user implies household finance data exists.
- **Recommendation:** Cap the array at about 10, rate-limit per auth.uid() (a counter table or pg function), and add a sliding-window quota. Consider returning only names for emails the caller has already added to one of their own shared_splits or households, or a boolean `has_account` without the name until the other party accepts. Test: an SQL test asserting that an 11-element call raises, and that the 21st call within a minute raises.
- **Verifier notes:** adversarial (confirmed): Sole definition 20260802120000_split_participant_name_resolution.sql:12-30: SECURITY DEFINER over auth.users join profiles, no auth.uid() scoping, no array length cap, granted to authenticated; comment admits 'pure directory lookup'. No later migration redefines it and no rate limit exists. Any self-registered user can bulk-map emails to display names.
- **Also reported as:** BE-FN-10 (Split email directory exposes who uses Vyact and other participants' emails); DB-SEC-12 (resolve_participant_names lets any signed-in user bulk-map emails to account existence an…); INT-HH-10 (resolve_participant_names reveals which emails have accounts, and their names, in unlimit…)
